## [SNN Training Reformulated as Convex Optimization with Global Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08022) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.08022v3) shows that the weight-decay-regularized training problem for a class of threshold recurrent neural networks, including leaky integrate-and-fire spiking neural networks, has zero duality gap and the same optimal value as a convex program. The authors introduce a practical randomized surrogate with a high-probability approximation guarantee, and report that the best convex variant outperforms surrogate-gradient training on addition and first-last-XOR, matches it on sequential MNIST, and trails a reservoir baseline on base-3 and base-5 addition. This addresses the long-standing non-convexity and non-differentiable spike function that prevent existing SNN training methods from guaranteeing a global optimum, potentially enabling more reliable and energy-efficient neuromorphic learning. It could influence how the AI/ML and neuromorphic computing communities approach SNN training, especially for tasks where global optimality and theoretical guarantees matter. The resulting convex programs can have exponentially many variables relative to the amount of data, which is why the authors introduce a randomized surrogate with a high-probability approximation guarantee rather than solving the convex program exactly. Empirically, the convex variant does not uniformly dominate: it trails a reservoir baseline on base-3 and base-5 addition, indicating task-dependent trade-offs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Spiking neural networks (SNNs) are artificial neural networks that mimic biological neurons by transmitting information as discrete spikes only when a neuron's membrane potential reaches a threshold, rather than at every propagation cycle. The leaky integrate-and-fire (LIF) model is the most prominent spiking neuron model, where the membrane potential integrates inputs and decays over time until firing. Because the spike function is non-differentiable and the training objective is non-convex, existing SNN training methods offer no guarantee of reaching a global optimum. Convex optimization is a class of problems where any local optimum is also global, and the duality gap measures the difference between the primal and dual optimal values; a zero duality gap means strong duality holds and the problem can be solved exactly via its convex dual.

## [FactoryNet: A 113M-Point Industrial Time-Series Dataset for Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09081) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers released FactoryNet, a pretraining corpus of 113 million datapoints drawn from 57,000 end-to-end task executions (15,000 real and 42,000 synthetic) across seven machine embodiments and four tasks, annotated with 27 anomaly types plus healthy baselines and counterfactual pairs. Every signal is mapped into a single control-theoretic schema — Setpoint, Effort, Feedback, Context (S-E-F-C) — so commanded intent and measured response become comparable across different machines. Industrial time-series research has lacked a large, well-annotated, cross-embodiment corpus, which has held back pretrained foundation models for machinery; FactoryNet fills that gap and enables cross-embodiment comparison, transfer studies, and anomaly-detection benchmarking. Its findings on simulation-to-real transfer and fault signatures give the field concrete evidence about what simulation can and cannot teach industrial models. A task-phase model trained purely on simulation, with no real phase labels, recovers roughly two-thirds of the real-data ceiling on both robots, and transfer holds within a robot family but rarely across manufacturers. Fault behavior transfers poorly: an uncalibrated simulator yields no detectable fault signal on either robot, and aligning feature distributions does not fix this — only the Effort channel (motor current) separates faults, and calibrating the simulator to reproduce that channel recovers a payload fault on the real robot.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Time-series foundation models, such as Google's TimesFM, Amazon's Chronos, and Salesforce's MOIRAI, aim to learn reusable representations that can be fine-tuned for forecasting and anomaly detection across domains. Industrial machines are actuated systems, meaning they receive commanded signals and produce measured responses, but most open datasets record only sensor outcomes or expose commands in vendor-specific formats, preventing comparison across machines. FactoryNet addresses this by imposing a common control-theoretic representation, where Setpoint is the commanded target, Effort is the actuation applied, Feedback is the measured response, and Context captures operating conditions.

## [Video Diffusion Models Internally Encode Physical Plausibility, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05328) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers at arXiv (2606.05328v2) inverted the deterministic sampling process of video diffusion models by integrating the learned velocity field backward from a clean video latent to noise, recovering intermediate states and attention maps. They found that physical plausibility is linearly decodable from diffusion transformer states across the IntPhys and InfLevel benchmarks, achieving around 81.27% average accuracy and outperforming dedicated representation-learning baselines such as V-JEPA and VideoMAE. This finding suggests that video diffusion models may serve as world simulators by internally encoding physical structure, not merely reproducing training motion patterns. It impacts AI/ML researchers working on world models, generative simulation, and representation learning, and implies that physically meaningful representations can emerge as a byproduct of generative denoising without explicit self-supervised objectives. The physical plausibility signal is absent from the VAE latent input and emerges inside the denoising transformer itself, despite the model not being trained with a self-supervised predictive objective. The study uses an approximate inversion technique to access intermediate states and attention maps, and the 81.27% average accuracy is measured across the IntPhys and InfLevel benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Video diffusion models are generative models that learn to denoise videos by reversing a forward diffusion process, and they have recently been proposed as candidate world simulators. Diffusion transformers (DiTs) replace the traditional U-Net backbone with a transformer architecture, using self-attention to capture global structure. IntPhys and InfLevel are benchmarks designed to test intuitive physics reasoning in AI systems by distinguishing physically possible from impossible events. Representation-learning baselines like V-JEPA and VideoMAE are trained with self-supervised predictive objectives to learn video representations.

## [CircuitLasso: Scalable Sparse Regression for LLM Circuit Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16939) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.16939v2) introduces CircuitLasso, a circuit learning method that uses sparse linear regression over sparse autoencoder (SAE) features to recover interpretable circuits in large language models. It matches the structural accuracy of state-of-the-art intervention-based methods on benchmark data while operating at a fraction of the computational cost. Circuit learning has been bottlenecked by the high dimensionality of SAE features, which makes intervention-based methods computationally prohibitive at scale. By turning circuit discovery into a sparse regression problem, CircuitLasso could make mechanistic interpretability practical for larger models and broader research use. CircuitLasso efficiently uncovers relationships among SAE features, showing how human-interpretable semantic features propagate through the model and influence predictions. The authors also validate the learned circuits on a domain-generalization task, achieving comparable performance at substantially lower cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer the internal computations of neural networks, but raw neurons are polysemantic—each responds to many unrelated concepts—making learned circuits hard to interpret. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) address this by learning sparse, more monosemantic features, but their high dimensionality makes existing intervention-based circuit learning methods computationally expensive. CircuitLasso applies sparse linear regression (lasso) to this problem, replacing intervention-heavy causal estimation with a scalable regression approach.

<a id="item-43"></a>
## [Output Forgetting Is Not True Forgetting in Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25001) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.25001v2) shows that machine unlearning methods judged successful by output-level metrics still leave structured residuals in representation space compared to models retrained from scratch without the forget data. The authors identify three signatures of this mismatch: forget/retain asymmetry, directional mismatch, and concentrated residuals along retraining-related directions. This finding suggests current unlearning evaluations may systematically overestimate success, which could mislead privacy, copyright, and safety applications that rely on unlearning to truly remove data influence. It is likely to push the community toward representation-level and retraining-consistent evaluation protocols. The study uses a population of retrained models as an operational reference distribution for ideal forgetting, and finds that in many cases the discrepancies exceed the variability of independent retraining, leading to a risk of information recovery during relearning attacks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove the influence of specific training data from a model without full retraining, and is typically evaluated by output-level metrics such as low accuracy on the forget set or reduced membership inference attack success. Representation space refers to the internal latent features a model learns, which may retain information not visible in outputs. Retraining from scratch without the forget data is considered the gold standard for ideal unlearning.

## [Audit Finds LLM Benchmarks Ignore Modality, Search, and Run Variance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06202) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.06202v2) audits a widely-used LLM by comparing ChatGPT's chat UI against OpenAI's API, with and without web search, using 401 stratified prompts from the BBQ and SafetyBench benchmarks and 4,812 total responses across three repeated runs per prompt. It finds that chat UI responses were less accurate than API responses with search disabled, that enabling web search cut accuracy by up to 8 percentage points and even reversed the modality trend on one benchmark, and that up to 21% of prompts produced inconsistent answers across repeated runs. The findings suggest that common benchmark practices — testing only through APIs, running each prompt once, and reporting a single accuracy number — can hide behavioral variation that matters for AI safety and deployment readiness claims. This could push benchmark designers and safety evaluators to standardize multi-run, multi-modality, and search-aware protocols. Beyond accuracy, the study measured response consistency, text similarity, citation grounding, and abstention behavior, finding that the two modalities grounded answers in different citations and that abstention was inconsistent across modalities. The sample was limited to 401 prompts from two benchmarks and one model family, so the specific magnitudes may not generalize to other models or tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: BBQ is a hand-built bias benchmark for question answering that uses ambiguous and disambiguated contexts to test whether models fall back on social stereotypes, while SafetyBench is a multiple-choice benchmark of over 11,000 questions covering seven categories of safety concerns. Benchmark scores are routinely cited as evidence that a model is safe and reliable enough to deploy, yet they are usually collected through a single interface and a single run per prompt. Citation grounding refers to whether a model's answer is actually supported by the sources it cites, and abstention refers to a model declining to answer when it lacks sufficient information.

## [New Metric Measures Whether LLM Chain-of-Thought Matches Internal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38972) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.38972) proposes CoT-Interpretability Alignment (CIA), a metric that measures how well an LLM's chain-of-thought explanations agree with its internal reasoning strategies as detected by interpretability tools. Evaluating three LLMs on two-hop question answering, hint intervention, and integer multiplication, the authors find only limited alignment (44.8–75.9%), but show that post-training with task accuracy and parametric faithfulness rewards can substantially improve alignment while maintaining or improving accuracy. Chain-of-thought traces are widely used as explanations for model behavior, so evidence that they often diverge from internal computation undermines trust in LLM transparency and AI safety auditing. This work offers both a quantitative framework for auditing CoT faithfulness and a practical post-training pathway toward making models' stated reasoning more trustworthy. The CIA metric compares CoT traces against internal reasoning strategies detected by interpretability tools, and the reported baseline alignment ranges from 44.8% to 75.9% across three tasks and three LLMs. The paper also analyzes generalization patterns of the improved faithfulness and releases code and data at https://github.com/yihuaihong/CIA-minimal-repro.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting asks a model to produce step-by-step reasoning before its final answer, and these traces are often treated as a window into how the model reached its conclusion. However, prior work such as Anthropic's 2023 faithfulness study and subsequent research has shown that CoT text can be altered without changing the final answer, suggesting it may be post-hoc rationalization rather than a faithful explanation. Interpretability tools attempt to detect the actual internal strategies a model uses, and this paper's CIA metric quantifies the gap between those internal strategies and the model's stated CoT.

## [Plug-in balcony solar panels rise as installation and regulatory hurdles grow](https://www.nationalgeographic.com/environment/article/balcony-plug-in-solar-panels-renewable-energy) ⭐️ 7.0/10

National Geographic reports on the growing popularity of plug-in solar panels, especially balcony solar systems that plug directly into a wall outlet instead of requiring professional installation and grid interconnection approval. These systems sidestep many of the bureaucratic and cost barriers that have made traditional rooftop solar inaccessible to renters and apartment dwellers. As solar panel costs continue to fall, installation labor and regulatory approval have become the dominant bottlenecks for residential solar adoption. Plug-in systems could dramatically expand access to renewable energy for renters and apartment residents who cannot install rooftop panels, potentially reshaping the residential solar market. Plug-in solar systems typically use grid-tie inverters with anti-islanding protection that shuts down output when the grid fails, and they are often installed vertically on balconies, which shifts peak generation to the edge of the typical 'duck curve.' In the US, the National Electrical Code does not explicitly address plug-in solar, leaving room for varied state-level interpretation, and power output is often limited to a fraction of a single circuit's capacity to avoid overloading wiring without tripping breakers.

**Background**: Traditional residential solar requires professional installation, permits, and a formal interconnection agreement with the local utility, which can add thousands of dollars and months of delay. Balcony or plug-in solar systems are small kits, typically one or two panels, that connect to a standard wall outlet through a microinverter, feeding power directly into home wiring. Anti-islanding is a critical safety feature that prevents the inverter from energizing the grid during an outage, protecting utility workers. The 'duck curve' refers to the daily pattern of net electricity demand that dips during midday solar production and rises sharply in the evening.

## [Equal-Budget Study: Randomized-Restart List Scheduling Beats MCTS for DAG Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12542) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A preregistered, controlled equal-budget study (arXiv:2610.12542) compared Monte Carlo Tree Search against a randomized-restart list scheduler for DAG workflow scheduling, giving all arms the same number of evaluated candidate schedules. At the main budget of 2048 schedules, MCTS was 2.88% worse than the restarts (95% CI [-3.62%, -2.15%]), and swapping in textbook UCT removed most but not all of the deficit (-0.26%, 95% CI [-0.71%, +0.23%]). The result challenges the common assumption that MCTS is a strong default for DAG workflow scheduling, showing that a much simpler randomized-restart list scheduler can win under equal search effort. It offers a cautionary empirical lesson for scheduling researchers and practitioners who may adopt tree search without running the simple equal-budget baseline. The deficit is not budget starvation: on a separate exploration pool, restarts self-improved by 1.85% while MCTS gained only 0.24%, and 700 of 720 MCTS first results were merely the warm start it was handed. The studied selection rule locks deterministically onto the first-expanded child, keeping trials correlated, and the causal link to makespan remains an inference; results are confined to a synthetic domain with 8-32 tasks, 2-8 processors, and budgets up to 2048.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) is a heuristic search algorithm that builds a decision tree using random simulations, and it became famous for mastering games like Go; its UCT variant balances exploration and exploitation. List scheduling is a classical, much simpler approach that assigns tasks to processors by priority rules, and a randomized-restart version simply reruns it with different random choices. DAG workflows are directed acyclic graphs of tasks with dependencies, common in data pipelines and scientific computing, where the goal is to minimize makespan (total completion time).

## [EvoSyn Evolves Generation Space for Closed-Loop Agent Data Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12593) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12593) introduces EvoSyn, a learning-feedback-driven multi-agent framework that organizes synthetic data generation around a revisable state specifying which interaction factors can be deliberately generated and where synthesis effort should be allocated. Experiments on τ²-Bench and CAR-Bench show EvoSyn improves average task success by 13.3 points over the strongest synthesis baseline while producing higher-quality, structurally broader synthetic experience. This work addresses the feedback-to-decision gap in closed-loop learning for LLM agents, where observed weaknesses do not directly specify how future synthesis should change. By translating learner feedback into concrete synthesis revisions, EvoSyn could make synthetic experience generation more targeted and effective, benefiting researchers working on agent training and data synthesis pipelines. The framework uses a role-specialized multi-agent system with a Designer that allocates synthesis directions and a Planner that grounds them into executable experience, enabling both within-space adaptation toward weak regions and synthesis-space expansion that promotes newly discovered factors into explicit generation controls. The abstract notes that EvoSyn discovers controllable factors that would be missed without learner feedback.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models increasingly rely on synthetic experience for continued improvement, but for interactive agents the synthesized data must be executable, verifiable, and pedagogically valuable to the current task agent. Closed-loop learning frameworks integrate feedback-driven computation with adaptive re-planning, yet existing synthesis methods often incorporate environment feedback or learner failures without a clear mechanism to convert those observations into concrete changes in the synthesis process. EvoSyn is positioned as a multi-agent solution to this gap, using a revisable generation space to guide what data gets created.

## [SpireBench Tests 20 LLMs on Long-Horizon Strategy in Slay the Spire](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12596) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SpireBench, a new benchmark in which 20 large language models play the complete commercial deck-builder Slay the Spire at its highest difficulty. Frontier models beat heuristic baselines and matched the average human player, but remained far behind expert players, with the gap widening as runs lengthened. Most existing agentic benchmarks are short and episodic, so this work offers an unsaturated, long-horizon evaluation that can dissect where and why agents fail over extended decision sequences. It matters for AI/ML researchers studying strategic reasoning, planning, and memory in agentic models. The strongest models win the first act as often as experts but fall behind afterward, and all models misjudge risk over a run by preserving health rather than spending it to grow stronger. A comprehensive renaming of every game term showed that part of their performance rests on prior knowledge of the game, and models did not improve from the memories they wrote across runs, which were often wrong and overly cautious.

**Background**: Slay the Spire is a commercial roguelike deck-building game in which players select a character, play cards to defeat monsters, and gradually build a deck across a run, so early decisions strongly determine long-term success. Long-horizon reasoning refers to an agent's ability to perform multi-step, temporally extended planning where dependencies span long time horizons and feedback is delayed. SpireBench is presented as the first harness to give a model the complete original game, and it is compared with existing harnesses.

## [Behavioral Self-Rewarding Improves RLVR for Software Engineering Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12598) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12598) introduces Behavioral Self-Rewarding (BSR), a reward-refinement method that reuses the policy itself to compare observable debugging behaviors among trajectories that share the same verifier outcome, converting pairwise preferences into bounded auxiliary rewards. Experiments on SWE-bench Verified and SWE-bench-Live show consistent improvements for Qwen3-14B and Qwen3-32B configurations. Binary pass/fail rewards cannot distinguish trajectories with substantially different debugging quality, so BSR offers a way to extract richer learning signals from already-collected trajectories without new environment interactions or a separately trained reward model. This could make reinforcement learning for code-generation agents more sample-efficient and effective. BSR aggregates pairwise preferences into task-local scores and converts them into bounded auxiliary rewards, introducing reward contrast while preserving the verifier-defined pass/fail ordering. The method is evaluated only on Qwen3-14B and Qwen3-32B models and remains an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) trains language models using automatic, rule-based checkers such as unit tests instead of learned reward models or human raters, which makes it scalable for tasks like coding. In software engineering agents, however, the verifier typically returns only a binary pass/fail signal, discarding information about how well a task was debugged. Self-rewarding approaches attempt to let a model judge its own outputs to expand reinforcement learning to domains where reliable external rewards are scarce.

## [CLEAT Teaches LLM Agents When to Ask Clarifying Questions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12655) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced CLEAT (CLarification through valuE-guided Agent co-Training), a framework that co-trains a clarification-value controller alongside the task policy using paired rollouts from the same interaction history. With Qwen3-4B, CLEAT achieved the highest mean score across all ten evaluation settings on four benchmarks, beating the strongest baseline by 13.2 percentage points on UserGym Travel and 6.5 points on IN3 underspecification judgment accuracy. Clarification is only useful if the agent can convert an answer into better decisions, so a method that learns to predict the utility of asking could substantially improve how LLM agents handle underspecified user intent. This matters for any interactive agent deployed in real-world settings where users omit goals or constraints, and it offers a general recipe for aligning agent behavior under incomplete information. CLEAT formulates a residual query value that measures the task utility gained by asking rather than proceeding under the current policy, estimated through paired rollouts that share the same interaction history. Ablations show that co-training improves the task policy even when the controller is removed at inference, and that adapting clarification guidance to the evolving policy yields further gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents can execute increasingly complex tasks, but users often leave goals and constraints unstated, creating a problem known as interactive intent alignment. Co-training is a machine-learning technique in which two models iteratively learn from each other's outputs, and paired rollouts are a reinforcement-learning trick that compares trajectories sharing the same conditions to reduce noise in reward comparisons. CLEAT combines these ideas so that a value controller predicts when clarification is worthwhile while the task policy learns to ask useful questions and act on the answers.

## [LLM Pipeline Builds Faithful Hierarchical Hardware Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12762) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.12762) presents an automated LLM-driven pipeline that converts hardware datasheets into hierarchical hardware knowledge graphs using chunk-based property extraction, hierarchical routing, and iterative refinement. Evaluated on 20 datasheets, it achieves 98.8% property faithfulness versus 76.1% for a one-shot baseline, and fully covers 83.6% of faithful baseline properties while the baseline covers only 10.5% of its properties. Hardware datasheets are lengthy and heterogeneous, and naive one-shot LLM processing omits relevant specifications even when the document fits in the context window, risking overlooked constraints and incorrect design decisions. This framework offers more reliable, source-grounded engineering information for component selection, system integration, and hardware compatibility verification, benefiting hardware engineering and knowledge graph communities. The staged design reduces per-query context and maintains traceable intermediate representations by preserving source provenance and applying graph updates through validated instructions and deterministic local execution. The paper introduces inter-graph completeness to measure relative semantic coverage without exhaustive ground truth, and it is an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion.

## [Aligning Unimodal Encoders with Only 100-500 Paired Examples via Distilled Optimal Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12781) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12781) introduces a method that aligns two independently pretrained unimodal encoders using only N ~ 100-500 paired examples, while leveraging abundant unpaired data on either side. The approach represents each representation by its similarities to the N paired anchors, matches the two point clouds with a single entropic optimal transport problem over the entire pool, and distills the resulting plan into two lightweight inductive encoders. This work addresses a practical bottleneck in cross-modal representation alignment, where collecting millions of paired examples is often expensive or impossible, and could enable alignment in domains like molecule-text where paired data is scarce. It outperforms existing approaches at the same anchor budget on Flickr30k, MS-COCO, and ChEBI-20, suggesting broad applicability. The method's marginal constraints counteract hubness, a phenomenon where certain points become nearest neighbors to many others, which typically defeats nearest-neighbor matching; the authors also relate this marginal constraint to one-step hub corrections from bilingual lexicon induction. Pairing is required only for the anchors, meaning no item outside the anchor set needs both modalities observed, and the approach is evaluated on image-text and molecule-text alignment tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Optimal transport is a mathematical framework for comparing and matching probability distributions, and its entropic variant adds a regularization term that makes the problem computationally tractable and yields unique solutions. Hubness is a common issue in high-dimensional nearest-neighbor search where some points act as hubs and attract many matches, degrading alignment quality. Bilingual lexicon induction is the task of inducing word translations from monolingual corpora, and its hub-correction techniques inspire the marginal constraints used here. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a complex model to a lightweight one, which in this work makes the alignment inductive so it can generalize to new unpaired data.

## [EvoBelief: Falsifiable World Models for Self-Improving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12789) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoBelief introduces a self-evolving world model that represents environment knowledge as falsifiable hypotheses, allowing an LLM agent to form, validate, and revise rules during deployment while the underlying language model stays frozen. On ALFWorld it improves success over a matched Frozen World Model baseline by 23.9% with Claude Haiku 4.5 and 31.3% with GPT-5.4, and in DiscoveryWorld's Reactor Lab it completes 83.3% of Normal-tier and 58.3% of Challenge-tier instances versus 0% for all evaluated baselines. This work shifts self-improvement for LLM agents away from action-centric reflection and skill accumulation toward evolving testable beliefs about environment dynamics, which could make agents more robust when their assumptions are incomplete, incorrect, or outdated. Because the language model remains frozen and each rule stays linked to its evidence, the approach also offers a more auditable and safety-friendly path to agent adaptation than parameter updates. Unexpected outcomes and decision-relevant unknowns trigger hypothesis formation, and later observations or controlled experiments provide independent validation before a rule can guide predictions or plans within its supported scope. Counterexamples narrow or retire rules as the environment changes, and the method is evaluated across four benchmarks spanning static dynamics, hidden drift, and scientific discovery.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: A world model in AI is an internal representation of an environment that predicts how the environment changes in response to actions. Falsifiability is the scientific standard that a claim must be testable and could in principle be shown false by counterexamples. Self-evolving LLM agents typically improve through accumulated experience, reflection, or skills, but even procedurally capable agents can fail when their beliefs about environment dynamics are wrong. EvoBelief combines these ideas by treating environment knowledge as falsifiable hypotheses that evolve during deployment without updating model parameters.

## [VEPA: Verified Evolving Formal Policy for Lifelong LLM Agent Guardrails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12813) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VEPA introduces a framework that represents LLM agent guardrails as formal policies of executable symbolic rules over typed agent events, allowing an LLM guided by deployment feedback to propose policy patches that a deterministic verifier then accepts only if they are structurally valid and conflict-free. An evidence-aware authority layer activates a verified rule only when it has sufficient supporting evidence, and VEPA consistently outperforms baselines across ASSEBench-Safety, ASSEBench-Security, and ATBench, including under mixed streams, corrupted feedback, and longer horizons. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent safety by combining deterministic formal verification with adaptive policy evolution, offering inspectable, reproducible, and deterministic allow-or-refuse decisions before potentially irreversible agent actions execute. It is likely to be of high interest to researchers and practitioners in AI safety, formal methods, and production agent deployment who need guardrails that both stay safe and keep adapting to newly observed risks. VEPA's design separates policy proposal (LLM-driven, feedback-guided) from policy acceptance (deterministic verifier checking structural validity and conflict-freedom) and policy activation (evidence-aware authority layer), so uncertain updates cannot directly affect guardrail decisions. The reported gains hold even under challenging deployment conditions such as mixed streams, corrupted feedback, and longer horizons, though the abstract does not specify latency overhead or the exact evidence thresholds used.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents increasingly operate over sensitive resources such as private documents and mutable system states, so runtime guardrails are needed to make allow-or-refuse decisions before irreversible actions are executed. Existing adaptive guardrails learn from deployment experience but apply policies through LLM inference, which can yield inconsistent decisions and ambiguous policy interpretations, while formal guardrails enforce policies deterministically but typically rely on pre-deployment or task-specific policies with no mechanism to adapt to newly observed risks. VEPA aims to bridge these two approaches by evolving formal policies in a verified manner.

A new arXiv paper (2610.12845) proposes formal postulates and a conceptual mathematical definition of creativity for AI applications, grounded in psychological theory and Boden's three criteria of newness, value, and surprise. Computational creativity has long lacked a unified, empirically testable definition, which hampers both evaluation and comparison of AI systems across art, science, and games. A validated mathematical formula could give researchers and developers a common metric for measuring machine creativity and for building AI systems that are explicitly optimized for creative output. The definition is built on three fundamental criteria—newness, value, and surprise—whose specific measurements are adapted to each domain, meaning the formula is not a single fixed metric but a structure parameterized by context. The validation is limited to two case studies (chess moves and paintings), so broader applicability to other creative domains remains to be demonstrated.

## [RGR Regularization Mitigates Capability Degradation in LLM Self-Improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12855) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12855) diagnoses capability degradation in on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) as a collapse of hidden representation geometry, marked by reduced intra-cluster compactness and diminished inter-cluster separation. The authors propose Representation Geometry Regularization (RGR), which preserves this geometry during post-training and can be integrated into existing OPSD methods without changing their core optimization procedures. This matters because OPSD is a widely used self-improvement paradigm where a model acts as both teacher and student, yet it can silently erode previously acquired capabilities even as overall performance rises. RGR offers a targeted, drop-in fix that could make LLM post-training more reliable for practitioners relying on self-distillation pipelines. The degradation is characterized by queries that were correctly answered early in training becoming incorrect as optimization proceeds, even while aggregate metrics improve. RGR works by encouraging compactness within clusters and separation across clusters, and the authors report extensive experiments across multiple OPSD methods and benchmarks showing it generally alleviates degradation while retaining capability gains; code is available at https://github.com/supermachinelearner/RGR.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) is a self-improvement paradigm in which a large language model uses itself as both teacher and student under different conditioning contexts, distilling token-level supervision along its own generated trajectories. This differs from classic knowledge distillation, where a separate teacher model supervises a student on static data. Representation geometry refers to the structure of hidden-layer embeddings, where intra-cluster compactness means similar inputs map to nearby points and inter-cluster separation means dissimilar inputs map to distant regions.

## [CToM Trains LLMs to Predict User Replies and Self-Revise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12872) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Collaborative Theory of Mind (CToM), a framework that trains LLMs to draft a response, predict how the user will reply, and use that prediction to internally revise the answer before presenting it. Combining a user-response prediction objective with group-relative policy optimization (GRPO), CToM raises final accuracy on a collaborative math reasoning task from 74.1% to 79.6% over naive GRPO, while cutting user replies by 8% on coding and 17% on writing. Multi-turn collaboration remains a weak point for LLMs, which often jump to solutions before understanding user needs or fail to correct mistaken assumptions after feedback. By explicitly training models to anticipate user reactions, CToM points toward more efficient and effective human-AI collaboration, which matters for any product built on extended dialogue such as coding assistants, tutors, and writing tools. During training, the user's actual reply to each draft serves as the prediction target, and the assistant minimizes the negative log-likelihood of that reply; this prediction objective is combined with GRPO to optimize both task performance and interaction efficiency. Ablations show that under the same reward, adding prediction supervision reduces user replies while improving task performance, and behavioral analysis finds that more accurate user-response predictions correlate with larger gains from self-revision.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Theory of Mind (ToM) refers to the ability to infer others' unobservable mental states, and prior work has tested whether LLMs exhibit ToM-like behavior on false-belief tasks. GRPO (group-relative policy optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm popularized by DeepSeek-R1 that compares groups of sampled outputs to estimate advantages without a separate value model. Multi-turn dialogue research shows LLMs often degrade in reasoning accuracy and lose track of information across extended interactions, which is the problem CToM targets.

## [Seq-Drifting Enables One-Step Text Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12879) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.12879) introduces Seq-Drifting, a framework that learns to map Gaussian noise directly to an entire text sequence in a single forward pass. It applies drifting to continuous token representations with context-adaptive attraction and repulsion, achieving a generative perplexity of 36.01 on LM1B in one step. Diffusion language models currently need many sampling steps to produce high-quality text, so a one-step method could dramatically cut inference cost and latency. If it generalizes, Seq-Drifting could make diffusion-based text generation competitive with autoregressive models in speed while retaining parallel generation. Unlike existing diffusion language models that derive supervision at each position directly from the observed token, Seq-Drifting builds a drift field whose position-wise targets adapt to the currently generated context. Attraction pulls outputs toward contextually plausible token representations, while repulsion prevents collapse across samples and repetition within sequences; the code is available at https://github.com/jyliu-ai/Seq-Drifting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion language models generate tokens in parallel by iteratively denoising a noisy representation, but they typically require multiple sampling steps to reach high quality. Drifting here refers to a training technique that shapes a vector field with attraction and repulsion forces, not the data-drift concept about distribution shift in deployed models. Seq-Drifting combines these ideas by treating text as continuous token representations and learning a direct noise-to-text map.

## [GeoSpatialBench Exposes a Spatial Gap in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12881) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced GeoSpatialBench, a new benchmark that evaluates multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on an escalating progression of spatial cognition, from spatial relation understanding and geometric reasoning to constrained spatial editing, using real-world urban data from six geographically diverse cities. The benchmark covers a taxonomy of 28 topological, directional, metric, and ordering relations across structured GeoJSON and map image modalities, with ground-truth references and constraints verified via GIS operations. Existing evaluations of MLLMs for spatial reasoning rely predominantly on static question-answering over small-scale or synthetic scenes, rarely probing whether models can modify spatial configurations while satisfying explicit geometric constraints. GeoSpatialBench fills this gap by providing an open, rigorous diagnostic testbed that lays empirical groundwork for physically grounded world models and embodied spatial AI. Empirical evaluation across frontier MLLMs uncovers a stark spatial gap: structured GeoJSON consistently outperforms map images, multimodal combinations offer no reliable gain over GeoJSON alone, and while models show reasonable competence on qualitative topological and ordering relations, performance drops on quantitative metric relations. Crucially, editing performance on ordering relations declines relative to QA, and most failed edits violate at least one specified spatial constraint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are AI systems that process both text and images, and spatial reasoning—understanding how objects relate in physical space—is considered fundamental to building grounded world models and embodied AI. GeoJSON is a standard JSON-based format (RFC 7946) for encoding geographic data such as points, lines, and polygons, widely supported by mapping libraries like Leaflet, Mapbox, and Google Maps. Prior spatial reasoning benchmarks have largely focused on static question-answering over synthetic or small-scale scenes, leaving constrained spatial editing underexplored.

## [OGPO: Offline Goal-Conditioned Policy Optimization for Recoverable VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12914) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose OGPO, an offline goal-conditioned policy optimization framework that post-trains vision-language-action (VLA) models by relabeling demonstration trajectories with semantic key states and constructing goal-conditioned process rewards, rather than relying on step-wise action imitation. Experiments on LIBERO and MetaWorld show consistent gains over standard supervised fine-tuning, and evaluations on LIBERO-Plus and the newly proposed LIBERO-ReAct perturbation benchmark demonstrate stronger robustness and goal-directed recoverability under distribution shifts and off-demonstration states. This work addresses a core limitation of trajectory-level supervised imitation in VLA post-training: policies trained this way tend to reproduce demonstration-specific action paths and lack guidance for recovering once execution deviates. By shifting post-training toward key-state reachability learning without requiring additional online interaction, OGPO could improve the reliability of robotic manipulation policies and offers a practical offline recipe for the broader VLA ecosystem. OGPO adaptively relabels offline demonstrations with semantic key states and optimizes the policy to reach these task-progress states using goal-conditioned process rewards, requiring no additional online interaction. The authors also introduce LIBERO-ReAct, a mid-execution perturbation benchmark designed to test recovery from recoverable off-demonstration states, though the work is an arXiv preprint without community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that take visual observations and natural language instructions as input and directly output low-level robot actions, typically built by fine-tuning a vision-language model on large-scale robot trajectory datasets. Their post-training still predominantly relies on trajectory-level supervised imitation, which supervises policies to reproduce expert actions at each step. Offline goal-conditioned reinforcement learning (GCRL) instead trains policies from fixed, pre-collected datasets to reach user-specified goals, and semantic relabeling uses language or semantic knowledge to identify task-critical key states as candidate goals. OGPO combines these ideas to make VLA policies more recoverable when execution deviates from demonstrations.

## [AI Shopping Assistants Reshape Retail Assortment and Revenue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12917) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12917) studies retail assortment planning when an AI intermediary forms a customer-facing shortlist, modeling both deterministic segment-specific rankings and stochastic mediation via the Plackett-Luce model. Using a dataset of product rankings from leading AI models, the authors find that ignoring AI mediation causes mean revenue losses of 2.52% to 11.37%, while a simple deterministic plug-in based on fitted AI utility orderings limits losses to at most 0.53%. As AI shopping assistants increasingly influence which products consumers consider, retailers that plan assortments without accounting for AI mediation may systematically leave revenue on the table. The findings give e-commerce platforms and AI system designers a concrete, quantifiable reason to incorporate AI ranking behavior into assortment optimization. The paper characterizes the computational complexity of assortment optimization under deterministic mediation and shows that, for any fixed number of customer segments, the problem admits a fully polynomial-time approximation scheme (FPTAS). The empirical pipeline estimates Plackett-Luce ranking models from AI model outputs and combines them with synthetic customer preferences to evaluate held-out assortments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Retail assortment planning is the problem of choosing which products to stock in order to maximize expected revenue, traditionally assuming customers choose directly from the retailer's catalog. The Plackett-Luce model is a statistical model for rankings that generalizes the Bradley-Terry pairwise comparison model, while the multinomial logit model predicts choice probabilities among multiple discrete alternatives. This paper inserts an AI intermediary between the retailer's catalog and the customer, so the retailer must optimize against the shortlist the AI produces rather than the full catalog.

A new arXiv paper (2610.12927) proposes collaborative learning over model slices, replacing a single large language model with smaller heterogeneous models under a comparable parameter budget. It introduces pyramid supervision, which combines protocol distillation with step-level supervision, and reports comparable or better performance than the original large model and multi-agent baselines across multi-hop QA, math reasoning, code generation, and open-ended constrained generation. Error propagation is a core reliability problem in LLM reasoning, where an early mistake cascades into a wrong final answer. By turning model heterogeneity into complementary reasoning capabilities, this approach could offer a more efficient alternative to multi-agent systems and influence how LLMs are deployed for complex reasoning tasks. The method uses model slicing to reassess prior reasoning across distinct model parameterizations, and pyramid supervision to refine reasoning and interaction decisions throughout collaboration. The trained heterogeneous models also achieve higher inference efficiency than the multi-agent baselines and show differentiated collaboration patterns across tasks.

Multi-agent baselines are systems where multiple models or agents interact to solve a task, often trained with reinforcement learning.

## [Uncertainty-Guided Contraction Cuts LLM Reasoning Training Cost 450x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12933) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12933) proposes an uncertainty-guided progressive contraction framework that compresses reasoning supervision for LLM post-training. Using Monte Carlo reasoning trees to expose process-level uncertainty, it reduces the optimization budget to as little as 0.22% of full-data SFT — over 450x less training exposure — while maintaining or improving reasoning performance across diverse benchmarks and model families. Scaling reasoning post-training with ever-larger corpora substantially increases training cost without proportional performance gains, so this work offers a path to data-efficient fine-tuning that could lower the barrier for researchers and organizations with limited compute. Its generalization across heterogeneous student families, black-box teachers, and weak-to-strong transfer suggests it may enhance reasoning capability rather than merely accelerate training. Instead of treating a teacher-generated chain-of-thought as a single realized path, the method builds Monte Carlo reasoning trees around intermediate reasoning states to expose process-level uncertainty. A first contraction distills the corpus into a compact teacher-informed pool, and a second learner-aware contraction performs online routing during SFT to concentrate optimization on the student's evolving learning state.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training refers to the stage after pretraining where techniques like supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning adapt a large language model to specific tasks such as complex reasoning. Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting elicits step-by-step reasoning traces, and Monte Carlo tree search — popularized by systems like AlphaGo — explores many possible reasoning paths by simulating random continuations from a given state. This paper combines these ideas to decide which reasoning trajectories are most worth learning from.

## [MathVis Benchmark Tests Whether LLMs Can Reason Through Diagrams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12954) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces MathVis, a benchmark of 512 tasks that evaluates LLMs' ability to visualize frontier mathematical problems and perform diagram-assisted reasoning, with each task including a statement, reference diagram, executable TikZ or SVG code, a Gold Intermediate Representation (IR), atomic rubrics, and error codes. Under rigorous evaluation, GPT-6-Astra, Claude Fable 5, and Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview scored pass@1 of only 31.25%, 26.56%, and 22.85% respectively. The results suggest that even the strongest current models largely fail to use diagrams as a genuine reasoning medium, which matters for AI research aiming to make LLMs solve geometry and other visual math problems more reliably. It also provides a rigorous, code-grounded evaluation framework that could steer future work on multimodal and diagram-mediated reasoning. Although the tasks demand visualization, the models' outputs are dominated by formal languages, symbols, and graphics programs rather than explicit diagram-mediated reasoning, and their solutions can contain all necessary elements yet still look visually confusing to humans. The benchmark relies on executable TikZ or SVG code and a Gold IR, so evaluation is grounded in whether the generated figure actually compiles and matches the intended structure.

An intermediate representation (IR) is an internal data structure used by compilers to represent a program in a form suitable for further processing, so a Gold IR here serves as a canonical, machine-checkable description of the intended figure. The pass@1 metric measures the fraction of tasks a model solves correctly on its first attempt, making it a strict indicator of one-shot capability.

## [SmartSalesBench: A Resistance-Aware Multi-Turn Benchmark for LLM Sales Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12975) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SmartSalesBench, the first multi-turn conversational benchmark designed to evaluate LLM sales agents against psychologically grounded, resistant buyer personas rather than cooperative users. It includes five agent profiles, twelve buyer personas, fifteen smartphone products across six price tiers, and four controlled experiments totaling 13,680 sessions, evaluated with nine metrics across three tiers. Existing task-oriented dialogue benchmarks assume cooperative users and only measure task completion, so they fail to capture how well agents handle resistance, objections, and pushback in realistic sales conversations. SmartSalesBench fills this gap and provides a large-scale, multi-metric foundation for evaluating and improving LLM sales and persuasion agents. Both sales-agent and buyer persona generations are grounded through retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), and evaluation combines automated LLM-as-judge scoring with internal human assessment (n = 50, kappa = 0.71, Pearson r = 0.81 with BRS). The nine metrics are grouped into primary outcome metrics (CR, BRS, RIR), secondary quality metrics (SMS, PFS, ETI), and exploratory metrics (ITS, ARI, SPI) planned for future reporting.

**Background**: Task-oriented dialogue benchmarks traditionally evaluate whether an agent can help a cooperative user complete a goal, such as booking a flight or finding a product. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique in which a language model first retrieves relevant documents from an external source and then uses that information to ground its responses, improving factual consistency. SmartSalesBench applies this grounding to both sides of the conversation and introduces resistant buyer personas, which are simulated customers programmed to challenge, object, and push back over conversations of up to twenty turns.

## [Continuous-Query Generation Bypasses Finite-Beam Limits in Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12977) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.12977) introduces two continuous-query generation paradigms, CQG-Single and CQG-AR, that replace discrete Semantic ID (SID) decoding in generative recommendation. CQG-Single generates one retrieval query in a single pass and outperforms the TIGER-style baseline in all seven runs while using only 55.3% as many trainable parameters, whereas CQG-AR autoregressively produces continuous residual updates and is numerically stronger on the Beauty and Sports datasets. The work identifies a fundamental reachability constraint in finite-beam SID decoding—held-out targets may never enter the returned list—and shows that continuous queries make every encoded item directly scoreable via dense or approximate-nearest-neighbor retrieval. This could shift how practitioners decouple output representation, autoregressive modeling, and retrieval budget when building LLM-based generative recommenders. The methods remove the SID item-token vocabulary, code lookup, and discrete beam expansion, thereby also avoiding the challenge of aligning a discrete SID vocabulary with the pretrained backbone's vector space. A controlled depth ablation shows that additional continuous refinement does not monotonically improve shallow accuracy, and the evaluation uses a unified protocol with valid-prefix constrained decoding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Generative recommendation (GR) typically represents each item as a Semantic ID—a sequence of discrete codes derived from embeddings—so that an LLM can autoregressively decode items like tokens. Because decoding uses finite-width beam search, only a limited set of candidate sequences is explored, which can make some relevant items unreachable. This paper asks whether replacing discrete SIDs with continuous queries removes that bottleneck while keeping the benefits of step-wise autoregressive decoding.

Researchers introduced Work2Skill, a framework that captures human GUI experience via synchronized screen recordings, mouse/keyboard events, and accessibility-tree data to induce reusable agent skills, alongside WorkDemo-350, described as the first synchronized multimodal dataset of 350 human desktop GUI demonstrations. An agentic skill-induction method then produces skills encoding workflow rules, step dependencies, and completion checks, improving mean scores by 4.67–10.54 points on MyPCBench Bounded Action and 3.11–7.50 points on a 286-task OSWorld-Verified subset over a No Skill baseline. By releasing both a framework and the WorkDemo-350 dataset, it provides a shared resource and evaluation baseline that could accelerate research on skill induction and GUI automation across the community. WorkRecorder aligns screen recordings, mouse and keyboard events, and available accessibility-tree information, and the 350 demonstrations were screened for completeness and task success. The skill-induction method inspects evidence on demand rather than processing everything at once, and the reported gains were measured when the same four execution models were equipped with skills under shared execution settings.

A new arXiv paper proposes Hindsight Structured Advice Distillation (HSAD), which trains a small advisor model to read each input and generate structured advice that is appended to the prompt of a frozen executor agent. On the FrontierFinance benchmark, an 8B advisor raised the qualification rate of Samaya's proprietary research agent from 52.9% to 56.6%, surpassing the agent's own high-effort configuration at 37% lower cost per query. The approach offers a practical way to improve capable but imperfect agents in specialized domains such as finance, healthcare, and law without retraining the executor, which is often impossible for closed or shared systems. Because the advisor is a separate small model, it is cheap to train and adds little latency, making it attractive for practitioners working with proprietary or shared agents. The advisor is trained via supervised fine-tuning using hindsight distillation with privileged information: a teacher that sees graded answers and execution traces writes the advice that would have helped, and the advisor learns to produce that advice from the input alone. The paper also reports generalization to WebShop, BIRD text-to-SQL, and ExpertLongBench, though the headline result is on a single financial benchmark.

Hindsight distillation with privileged information is a technique where a teacher model uses extra information available only during training to teach a student model that must act without it at inference time.

<a id="item-79"></a>
## [LQET Trains Language Models to Weight Evidence by Provenance, Not Repetition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13008) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.13008) introduces Latent Quotient Evidence Training (LQET), a relational training objective that combines sparse Bayesian supervision with constraints encouraging stable predictions when reports add no new evidence. On a controlled binary benchmark, LQET reduced new-evidence update RMSE by 41-44% and log-odds drift after eight relay steps by 89-91% compared to task-only training across Qwen3-4B and Phi-4-mini, with three paired training seeds per backbone. This work addresses a critical failure mode in human-AI feedback loops where a single observation can be quoted, summarized, and returned as multiple reports, making repetition look like independent corroboration. By grounding belief updates in supplied provenance, LQET offers a route toward dynamic human-AI systems in which influence follows new evidence rather than the number of times the same evidence returns, with implications for AI safety and reliability. The gains extend to a closed-loop simulation in which model confidence controls how many relayed reports re-enter the model's context in later rounds. However, the benchmark is controlled and the work is a preprint without peer review or community discussion, limiting immediate impact.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: In repeated human-AI interaction, language models often treat repeated information as independent corroboration, leading to overconfidence and belief drift. Bayesian methods provide a principled framework for updating beliefs based on evidence reliability and independence, but applying them to language models is challenging. Provenance refers to the lineage or source history of information, which can help distinguish genuinely new evidence from recycled reports.

<a id="item-80"></a>
## [EmbodiedCLBench: First Benchmark for Continual Learning in Embodied Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13021) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers from PKU Value Lab introduced EmbodiedCLBench, the first benchmark in embodied simulation designed to evaluate the continual learning capability of self-evolving agents, with compositional generalization as its core evaluation principle. The benchmark tests whether agents can learn from basic tasks and then recombine those skills to solve complex, unseen advanced tasks, and experiments across multiple agent harnesses and learning methods reveal consistent limitations. This work addresses a clear gap by extending continual learning research from non-embodied settings into embodied environments, which is critical for building agents that operate in dynamic, real-world scenarios. It provides a standardized evaluation resource that could shape how the embodied AI and robotics communities design and measure self-evolving agents. Experiments show that advanced tasks are hardly solvable in a zero-shot setting, but experience from basic tasks yields consistent improvement that remains stable even as task complexity increases. However, this improvement does not scale with the number of learning samples, indicating that current agents cannot extract meaningful information from additional experience; code and dataset are available at the project homepage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning refers to a model's ability to accumulate past experience and transfer it to new tasks without forgetting prior knowledge, a challenge often framed as avoiding catastrophic forgetting. Compositional generalization is the ability to recombine previously learned skills to handle novel, unseen combinations of tasks. Embodied agents are AI systems that perceive and act within simulated or physical environments, and self-evolving agents aim to autonomously improve their reasoning and behavior over time. Existing continual learning benchmarks have largely been confined to non-embodied settings, leaving embodied evaluation underexplored.

<a id="item-81"></a>
## [ReTRAIL: Rewinding Environments to Boost Long-Horizon LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13058) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced ReTRAIL, a framework that lets LLM agents rewind their environment to earlier states while preserving knowledge gained from abandoned exploration branches. It trains a shared policy using State-Anchored GRPO and a branch-advancement reward, and was evaluated on MineSweeper, Sokoban, ALFWorld, and ScienceWorld. Long-horizon tasks often suffer from cascading failures after early mistakes, and this work shows that simply adding a rewind primitive is insufficient—coupling it with experience reuse is key. This could improve the robustness of LLM agents in complex planning domains like robotics, web navigation, and interactive games. ReTRAIL uses State-Anchored GRPO to compare alternative continuations from revisited states and a branch-advancement reward to guide reflection based on subsequent exploration progress. Experiments show that adding a rewind primitive alone provides limited benefit and can even degrade performance, while ReTRAIL enables agents to recover from unproductive states.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model (LLM) agents are increasingly used for long-horizon tasks where they must make sequences of decisions over many steps. Reinforcement learning methods like GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) are used to train such agents by comparing groups of actions, but they often struggle with exploration and credit assignment over long horizons. ReTRAIL builds on these ideas by allowing agents to backtrack in the environment while retaining learned knowledge, similar to how humans might rewind a game to try a different strategy.

## [LongHarness Manages Evidence State for 100M-Token Agentic Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13075) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.13075) introduces LongHarness, a model-agnostic harness whose core mechanism, Evidence-Conclusion Coevolution, explicitly manages the interaction between acquired evidence and evolving conclusions to prevent unbounded exploration and evidence-state drift. Evaluated on OfficeQA Pro V1/V2, AA-LCR, and LongBench v2 with GLM-5.3-Flash and Qwen3.8-Flash, it raises GLM-5.3-Flash exact match on OfficeQA Pro from 15.8% to 60.2% on V1 and from 4.4% to 61.1% on V2, surpassing Claude Code by 12.0 points on V1 and Codex by 18.9 points on V2. Corpus-level reasoning over document collections far larger than any context window is a key bottleneck for enterprise and research agents, and LongHarness shows that explicit state management can deliver large accuracy gains without changing the underlying model. Its model-agnostic design means the same harness could be layered onto many existing LLM backbones, potentially making long-context agentic workflows more reliable and less dependent on ever-larger context windows. LongHarness couples Evidence-Conclusion Coevolution with Evidence Lifecycle Management, which keeps accumulated evidence current and traceable throughout the interaction so that retrieval focuses on remaining information needs and conclusions stay aligned with current evidence. The reported results are exact-match scores on OfficeQA Pro V1/V2, AA-LCR, and LongBench v2 using GLM-5.3-Flash and Qwen3.8-Flash; with Qwen3.8-Flash it reaches 61.7% on V1, outperforming Claude Code by 8.3 points and Codex by 12.0 points, though the work remains a preprint without independent replication.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Corpus-level long-context reasoning asks an AI agent to answer questions by gathering and integrating evidence from document collections that are far larger than the model's context window, so the agent must retrieve incrementally rather than read everything at once. Existing agent harnesses often let retrieved evidence and intermediate conclusions pile up in a growing interaction history, which can cause two coupled failure modes: unbounded exploration, where retrieval keeps expanding the history without closing decision-relevant information gaps, and evidence-state drift, where earlier evidence or later corrections fail to inform the current conclusion. LongHarness is a model-agnostic harness, meaning it wraps around an existing LLM rather than requiring a specific model, and it makes the relationship between evidence and conclusions explicit instead of leaving it implicit in the history.

## [Attention Recognizes Harm, MLP Writes Refusal in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13078) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.13078) traces an attention-to-MLP safety pathway in decoder-only Transformers, showing across 18 Base-Instruct model pairs (Qwen, Llama, Gemma) that harmfulness is more decodable from attention updates, especially in early layers, while MLP updates drive harm-selective refusal writing in aligned models. The authors also introduce Sentinel, a dual-signal method combining an attention-derived perception score P and an MLP-derived decision score D, which outperforms single-signal routing and fixed reminders across eight models. This work separates harmfulness recognition from refusal generation, offering a mechanistic explanation for why safety-aligned LLMs remain vulnerable to jailbreaks and prone to over-refusal. The Sentinel framework suggests a practical path toward more reliable safety routing by jointly using perception and decision signals, which could inform alignment and guardrail design. Post-training strengthens MLP refusal writing in all 18 pairs without systematically improving harmfulness decodability, and positive MLP writing becomes more concentrated in deeper layers. In six reasoning-enabled models, harmfulness stays decodable during reasoning while strong refusal writing concentrates at answer onset, and causal interventions show that resetting downstream MLP updates to clean values substantially reduces the refusal shift caused by upstream attention interventions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability is a subfield that reverse-engineers the internal computations of neural networks, often focusing on Transformer language models. In decoder-only Transformers, each sublayer (attention or MLP) proposes an update that is added to the residual stream, the running representation that carries information across layers. Safety alignment typically uses post-training such as instruction tuning or RLHF to make models refuse harmful requests, yet models still fail via jailbreaks or over-refusal. This paper asks which internal module recognizes harmfulness and which one actually writes the refusal.

## [Faster Decisions Don't Guarantee Better Agents: Jev Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13092) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13092) empirically evaluates integrating Jev, a fast decision layer from TypeSafe AI, into LLM-based agent workflows across 4,500 task executions using five agents in ALFWorld and AndroidWorld. Results show Jev substantially cuts decision cost and latency in all five agents, but task success stays near baseline on ALFWorld and declines notably in two mobile agents. This study challenges the common assumption that cheaper, faster decision-making automatically translates into better agent performance, showing that local efficiency gains can be absorbed by auxiliary reasoning, failed recovery, and premature completion. It matters for researchers and practitioners designing LLM agent systems, who must evaluate efficiency by successful task delivery and resources per delivered outcome rather than per-attempt savings. The evaluation spans five agents and two domains, jointly measuring task effectiveness, decision-level and task-level resource use, and execution behavior. Notably, mean cost per successful outcome increased in one agent even as spending per attempt fell, and the paper attributes failures to weak progress preservation, poor recovery, and insufficient completion verification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based agents rely on repeated decisions to complete tasks, with large language models typically serving as the core decision maker. Jev, from TypeSafe AI, is a fast decision layer designed to sit between intent and action, making small reliable branches faster and cheaper than full LLM generation. ALFWorld is a text-based embodied benchmark for navigation and manipulation tasks, while AndroidWorld tests agents on 116 tasks across 20 real mobile apps in a live Android emulator.

## [Black-Box Causal Audit Reveals Model-Dependent Audio Grounding in LALM Hallucination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13112) introduces a black-box causal test that localizes a claim's evidence region in audio via CLAP similarity, removes it with spectral gating, and compares against random-window controls across a 2x2x2 grid on three LALMs. On Qwen2-Audio-7B, localized ablation's raw AUROC (0.613) trails the blank-waveform baseline (0.721), but this is a duration confound; when duration is held fixed, localization beats random removal at every cell on both models, and a gated ensemble of blank and language-prior contrasts raises Qwen2-Audio accuracy from 57.1% to 71.9%. This work challenges the out-of-distribution critique of Audio-Aware Decoding (AAD) by showing that localized evidence removal can outperform blank-waveform baselines once duration is controlled, and it demonstrates that a unified causal-grounding profile does not transfer between models. The findings suggest that hallucination mitigation strategies must account for model-specific grounding regimes, with direct implications for AI safety and interpretability research on large audio-language models. The audit uses a 2x2x2 grid across three LALMs, with the blank baseline removing the entire clip versus the grid's 2-4 second windows, which explains the raw AUROC gap on Qwen2-Audio-7B. Detection value remains model-dependent, especially on Audio Flamingo 3, and the source code plus a detailed report of limitations are available at the project's GitHub repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large audio-language models (LALMs) can take audio and text as inputs and answer questions about the audio, but they frequently exhibit object hallucination, affirming sounds that are absent. Audio-Aware Decoding (AAD) is the dominant mitigation, contrasting logits under real audio against an all-zero "blank" waveform, but prior review flagged this blank contrast as out-of-distribution. CLAP (Contrastive Language-Audio Pretraining) is a model that learns acoustic concepts from natural language supervision and enables zero-shot inference, used here to localize evidence regions, while spectral gating is a signal-processing technique that removes targeted frequency content from audio.

## [KnowForge splits document QA into Locator, Judge, and Reader agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13128) ⭐️ 7.0/10

KnowForge introduces a role-specialized pipeline over a hybrid-retrieval knowledge-point index, with a search-only Locator agent, a code-scheduled bridge judge forcing a second retrieval hop, a typed evidence pool, a read-only Deep Reader agent, and a format-checking synthesizer. Using a self-hosted Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (3B active), it reaches F1 47.5 on the full open-domain M3DocVQA benchmark, +11.0 over the published M3DocRAG system and +7.1 over one-shot retrieve-and-answer on the same index and model, and 53.5 with DeepSeek-V4-Flash. The results suggest that separating retrieval from reading, rather than letting one model schedule both, materially improves open-domain multimodal document QA and multi-hop RAG accuracy, which could influence how future retrieval-augmented agents are architected. The paper also reports that merging both agents into one with all tools and twice the rounds loses 1.9 F1 because the model keeps searching instead of reading, a finding relevant to anyone building RAG systems. Per-job ablations attribute the gains to the Deep Reader, worth 3.8, 9.8, and 0 points on the three corpora, tracking how visual each corpus is; a round-budget sweep shows a step at four Locator rounds and a plateau beyond, and query latency scales with model calls rather than corpus size. On MultiHop-RAG it reaches 73.5 accuracy, above the strongest published RAG and GraphRAG results (71.2), and on single-document MMLongBench-Doc it lifts the same model from 34.8 to 47.0, level with GPT-4o reading page images (46.3).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique in which a large language model first retrieves relevant documents from an external source and then generates an answer grounded in them, reducing hallucinations and avoiding retraining. M3DocVQA is a benchmark for multi-modal, multi-page, multi-document understanding, with evidence distributed across thousands of PDFs, making it a hard test of open-domain document QA. KnowForge's knowledge-point index is a hybrid retrieval structure that combines dense and sparse signals to locate candidate evidence before specialized agents read it.
