Researchers introduced GeoFitBench-LLM, a benchmark that tests whether large language models can infer the datasets, supporting resources, and constraints needed for geo-analytic requests expressed in natural language. Experiments on seven models under minimal prompting showed GPT-5.4 Thinking performed best, recovering the complete set of resources in 66.67% of benchmark requests. This benchmark addresses a gap in GeoAI evaluation, where LLMs are increasingly used but their ability to elicit geospatial data requirements is rarely tested. It could help researchers and practitioners build more reliable retrieval-oriented geo-analytic systems by exposing where models still fail. The benchmark is organized around two reasoning dimensions, Spatio-Temporal Relational and Semantic Grounding, with ground truth encoded in a fixed, provider-independent schema for reproducible evaluation. While identifying necessary resources was often achievable, accurate fitness-for-use specification remained harder as inferential demands increased.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: GeoAI refers to the combination of geospatial data and artificial intelligence, such as machine learning and data mining, to better understand the physical world. Requirements elicitation is the early phase of identifying what data, actors, and conditions a task needs, and semantic grounding means linking concepts to canonical entities so that retrieval is meaningful. GeoFitBench-LLM applies these ideas to evaluate whether LLMs can specify the right geospatial datasets and constraints before a geo-analytic task is executed.

<a id="item-298"></a>
## [COFFEQA: Counterfactual-Guided Few-Shot Distillation for LLM QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12837) ⭐️ 6.0/10

COFFEQA introduces a counterfactual-guided few-shot distillation method for open-book question answering, using minimally modified contexts that flip the teacher's answer to provide extra supervision around hard factual samples. It also proposes a new metric, Context Following Difficulty (CFD), to validate counterfactuals before distillation, and reports gains of 8.77 average EM and 9.98 average F1 points over factual-only distillation on TriviaQA with T5 under a 32-pair few-shot budget. Few-shot distillation is attractive for deploying smaller models in resource-constrained settings, but its performance is highly sensitive to which samples are chosen. By showing that uncertain samples near the teacher's decision boundary can degrade distillation and by using counterfactuals to fix this, COFFEQA offers a practical way to get more out of very limited training data. A counterfactual here is a minimally modified context that induces the teacher to change its answer to an alternative one, and CFD validates such pairs by checking agreement with the intended alternative answer and dependence on the modified context. Experiments span five in-domain and three out-of-domain QA benchmarks using T5 and Qwen2.5 families, with geometric insights on how factual-counterfactual pairs constrain the deviation between teacher and student decision boundaries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation trains a smaller student model to imitate a larger teacher model, typically to compress models for resource-constrained deployment. Few-shot learning aims to learn a task from only a handful of examples, and few-shot distillation combines the two by transferring teacher knowledge with very limited samples. Counterfactual reasoning in AI explores 'what-if' scenarios by making minimal changes to inputs and observing how outputs change, which is used here to generate extra supervision signals.

## [Diffusion Model Optimizes Ship Hulls, But High-Speed Resistance Lags](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12870) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a reward-directed diffusion framework for localized ship hull optimization, using parametric masking to restrict changes to the bulbous bow and forebody while preserving full-hull continuity. The generated variant achieved comparable total resistance to a naval architect's refinement at low speed (Fn=0.195) but showed 45-50% higher resistance at higher speeds (Fn=0.250 and 0.260), a flaw missed by empirical screening. This work highlights both the promise and current limitations of applying generative AI to complex engineering design, showing that surrogate-based screening can miss critical performance tradeoffs at off-design conditions. It underscores the need for high-fidelity CFD assessment and more physically complete reward functions before AI-generated designs can match human expertise. The framework uses surrogate-assisted sequential Monte Carlo sampling to minimize predicted calm-water resistance, with stability maintained via training data and displacement matching during screening. An automated viscous free-surface CFD pipeline, validated on two benchmark container ships, revealed the generated hull's inability to represent a faired bulbous bow and a reward function lacking viscous pressure and stability terms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative AI techniques that learn to create new data by reversing a noise-adding process, and they have recently been adapted for engineering design tasks. In ship design, localized optimization focuses on specific regions like the bulbous bow to reduce resistance, which is crucial for fuel efficiency. Surrogate-assisted optimization uses fast approximate models to guide the search, but high-fidelity CFD simulations are needed for accurate performance evaluation.

## [MotionCoach: Multimodal Agent Guides Professional Motion Video Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12963) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce MotionCoach, a trained multimodal agent that uses multi-turn tools and self-verification to produce a grounded prompt and reference clip, injecting external professional motion knowledge into video editing models. It is cold-started on MotionCoach-SFT-10k and optimized with GRPO on MotionCoach-RL-2k, and a new benchmark VPMEBench with 314 professional motion editing tasks is released. Experiments show overall scores improve from 55.1 to 65.6 on Seedance 2.0 and from 51.2 to 64.5 on MiniMax-H3. Professional motions in sports, performance, and martial arts require fine-grained technique and temporal structure that general video editing models often fail to reproduce, as when a Stoppie instruction incorrectly lifts the front wheel instead of the back. MotionCoach shows that learning to construct multimodal guidance can enable precise professional motion editing without retraining the underlying video editing models, which could benefit creators and athletes who need accurate technique demonstrations. The agent is trained via a data pipeline that constructs trajectories, with cold-start supervised fine-tuning on 10k samples and reinforcement learning on 2k samples using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with rewards on the motion guidance prompt, the motion outcome video, and the format. Evaluation uses VPMEBench, comprising 314 professional motion editing tasks with fine-grained references and annotations, though the abstract is truncated and no community discussion is available.

## [GeoGR Cuts 3D VLM Inference Cost via Token Compression and Group Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13035) ⭐️ 6.0/10

GeoGR, a two-stage framework for multi-view 3D vision-language models, introduces GeoSemZip at the projector level to consolidate cross-view observations into compact 3D voxel tokens, and GroupRoute at the LLM level to skip low-relevance visual tokens. On LLaVA-OneVision-7B and Video-3D LLM-7B, it retains 95.2% and 96.8% of dense post-trained performance while accelerating LLM prefill by 6.8x and 6.1x, with code released on GitHub. Multi-view 3D VLMs suffer from long visual token sequences that make inference expensive, and GeoGR shows that jointly compressing scene representations and visual computation can deliver large speedups with minimal accuracy loss. This favorable performance-efficiency trade-off could make 3D scene understanding more practical for real-time and resource-constrained multimodal applications. GeoSemZip combines semantic saliency with spatial coverage and aggregates unselected voxel features into contextual anchors to retain otherwise discarded scene information. GroupRoute exploits locally stable query-conditioned token relevance by combining late entry, early exit, and recoverable group-wise skipping, where low-relevance tokens bypass complete LLM layers but keep hidden states and can rejoin at anchor layers under a computation budget; compression-aware post-training uses a compact 67K-sample dataset retaining 30% of the original training pool.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models such as LLaVA process images and text together, and extending them to 3D scenes typically requires feeding many multi-view images, which produces very long visual token sequences. Because the same scene regions are observed repeatedly across views and all visual tokens are processed at every LLM layer, inference becomes costly. Token compression and layer-skipping techniques aim to remove this redundancy, and voxel-based aggregation groups tokens by their 3D spatial location.

## [ECC: CTR-Driven Ad Image Editing via Attribute Disentanglement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13066) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose ECC (Evolving Creatives with Clicks), which reformulates advertising creative optimization as fine-grained attribute editing rather than generating images from scratch. ECC identifies 28 CTR-correlated attributes via ANOVA and Random Forest regression, instruction-tunes a prompt model on attribute contrasts between high- and low-CTR images, and introduces Consistent Preference Optimization (CPO) to mitigate DPO overfitting. Unlike prior CTR-reward methods that penalize all attributes of an underperforming ad equally, ECC selectively refines only CTR-hurting attributes while preserving beneficial ones, which could substantially improve e-commerce ad performance. It outperforms state-of-the-art models by a substantial margin, and the full data pipeline, dataset, and source code are promised upon acceptance. The attribute space is narrowed to 28 attributes significantly correlated with CTR using ANOVA and Random Forest regression, and editing suggestions are derived from attribute contrasts between images with disparate CTRs. CPO enforces consistency across semantically equivalent shuffled suggestions to improve preference generalization, though reference-derived suggestions are not uniformly beneficial, which is why real click preferences are used for refinement.

## [First Black-Box Uncertainty Estimation Study for AMR Parsers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13236) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13236) presents the first study of uncertainty estimation for Abstract Meaning Representation (AMR) parsers in a black-box setting, using top-k beam-search candidate similarities to estimate uncertainty and evaluating it with the Prediction Rejection Ratio (PRR). The work characterizes the relationship between parsing performance and uncertainty, evaluates the reliability of beam candidate distributions, and identifies parsers that best balance performance and reliability. AMR parsers have become highly accurate, but knowing when their outputs can be trusted remains largely unexplored; this work introduces a practical, black-box method to flag unreliable parses, which could improve downstream applications that depend on AMR graphs. It also adds uncertainty estimation as a complementary evaluation dimension for the AMR parsing subfield. The method is black-box, meaning it requires only the top-k beam-search candidates produced by a parser rather than access to model internals, and uncertainty is derived from pairwise similarities among those candidates. Evaluation uses the Prediction Rejection Ratio, which measures how well uncertainty estimates correlate with parsing errors, and the study also analyzes how different evaluation metrics affect uncertainty assessment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Abstract Meaning Representation (AMR) is a semantic representation language that encodes the meaning of a sentence as a rooted, labeled, directed acyclic graph, abstracting away from syntactic form. AMR parsing is the task of automatically producing such graphs from natural language, and recent neural parsers have reached high accuracy on standard benchmarks. Uncertainty estimation, a growing topic in NLP, aims to quantify how confident a model is in its predictions so that unreliable outputs can be detected or rejected. Beam search is a decoding strategy that keeps the top-k most likely candidate outputs, and comparing those candidates can serve as a signal of model uncertainty.

## [Sigmoid Label Enables Sub-Degree DOA Estimation in CNN-Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13433) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13433) proposes a sigmoid label encoding for CNN-Transformer networks that encodes source directions-of-arrival as sums of angle-shifted monotonic sigmoid functions. Combined with a 0.25-degree grid step, this approach achieves sub-degree accuracy and 1-degree resolution, overcoming the grid quantization error that limits conventional classification-based DOA methods. Grid quantization error has long constrained the accuracy and resolution of deep-learning-based DOA estimation, so this label-encoding technique could improve applications in radar, sonar, and wireless communications without requiring an extremely dense grid. It also offers a generalizable idea for other classification-based regression tasks in signal processing. The method differentiates the CNN-Transformer output to produce sharp spectral peaks at source DOAs, and under a unified backbone it outperforms Gaussian labels, 0-1 hard labels, and a binary cross-entropy baseline in both accuracy and resolution. The evaluation uses a moderately refined 0.25-degree grid step rather than an extremely fine grid.

Deep-learning-based DOA methods often treat the task as classification over a discretized angular grid, which introduces grid quantization error: accuracy and resolution are bounded by the grid step and the width of the label peak. CNN-Transformer hybrids combine convolutional local feature extraction with transformer self-attention for global context, and are increasingly used in such signal-processing tasks.

A new arXiv paper proposes a model-agnostic knowledge augmentation method that improves classification of weakly annotated bioimaging data across seven ML architectures. When combined with masked autoencoder-based self-supervised learning, it achieves up to 2.9% precision gains on three in-house microscopy datasets and an average of 6.8% across three public cytomorphology benchmarks. Weakly annotated bioimaging data is common in biomedical research, where pixel-level labels are expensive, so a model-agnostic method that consistently improves performance could make automated image analysis more reliable across labs and architectures. The authors apply the best model to nucleus-level classification of TDP-43 mislocalization from nucleus to cytoplasm in motor neurons derived from human induced pluripotent stem cells, revealing dose-dependent effects of MG132 and sodium arsenite, variable pathology across sporadic and familial ALS donors, and no protection from edaravone or niclosamide against SA-induced mislocalization.

## [Training Budgets, Not Architecture, Drive ARC Performance, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13660) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A controlled study on arXiv (2610.13660) systematically crossed five pretraining budgets with four test-time training (TTT) budgets for the VARC and TRM models on ARC-AGI-1, finding that increasing TTT sample presentations improves pass@2 for both models, while increasing the TTT task-variant count consistently helps only TRM and often hurts VARC. The results suggest that reported architecture advantages on ARC may be confounded by differing training and evaluation budgets, so the community should report results at comparable budgets to make fair claims about model design. Each budget is defined by a task-variant count and total sample presentations; notably, VARC remains competitive without any pretraining, whereas TRM benefits much more from pretraining, indicating the two architectures have different data and compute sensitivities.

## [Bilevel Stackelberg Formulation Jointly Trains Accurate and Calibrated Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13693) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13693) proposes a bilevel Stackelberg formulation that trains standard post-hoc calibrators end-to-end with the neural network backbone, eliminating the need for a separate calibration dataset or dedicated calibration loss. The authors show that using the final classification layer itself as the calibrator yields accurate, well-calibrated models with no additional parameters, outperforming state-of-the-art calibrators with minimal added complexity. Reliable uncertainty estimation is critical for deploying neural networks in safety-sensitive domains such as healthcare and autonomous driving, where miscalibrated confidence scores can lead to dangerous decisions. By removing the need for a separate calibration set and post-hoc optimization pipeline, this approach could simplify model development and reduce overhead, particularly in multi-task learning where separate calibration components introduce multiplicative costs. The framework supports calibrators ranging from temperature scaling and matrix scaling to neural network layers, but the key finding is that the final classification layer alone suffices as the calibrator, adding zero parameters. The only additional hyperparameter introduced is the calibrator learning rate, and the approach avoids the multiplicative overhead of separate calibration components in multi-task settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Neural networks are often poorly calibrated, meaning their predicted confidence scores do not reflect true correctness likelihoods, which is problematic for safety-critical applications. Post-hoc calibration methods address this by adjusting a trained model's outputs using a separate calibration dataset and additional parameters, but this adds overhead and requires separate optimization. Bilevel optimization, rooted in Stackelberg game theory, involves a leader and follower where the leader optimizes subject to the follower's optimal response, and this paper applies that structure to jointly optimize accuracy and calibration.

## [MCGPO Improves Credit Assignment in Long-Horizon LLM Agent Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13755) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes Matched-Context Group Policy Optimization (MCGPO), a method that constructs weighted action preferences from already-collected trajectories to improve credit assignment in long-horizon LLM agent training. Across six environment-backbone settings with three training seeds each, MCGPO improves mean success over matched GraphGPO by 0.47–1.46 percentage points on ALFWorld and WebShop with Qwen2.5-1.5B and 7B, and by 3.96 and 5.16 points on WebShop with Qwen3-4B and 8B. Credit assignment from sparse terminal feedback is a core bottleneck in training LLM agents that act over many turns, so methods that extract better learning signal from existing rollouts could broadly improve agentic reinforcement learning. MCGPO is notable because it reuses rollout data and preserves critic-free training, making it compatible with existing group-based policy optimization pipelines rather than requiring new infrastructure. MCGPO defines comparison contexts using pre-action state summaries and observed rubric progress annotated by task rules, selects among ordered exact and coarse matching rules via two-action support, and uses fixed folds to separate candidate decisions from terminal outcomes. Support-dependent evidence weights scale an auxiliary pairwise objective alongside shared GraphGPO task learning; gains on ALFWorld build on baseline success above 92%, where remaining headroom is limited, and integrated success improves in three of four Qwen2.5 settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon LLM agents interact with an environment over many turns and typically receive only a sparse terminal reward, making it hard to know which intermediate decisions were responsible for success or failure. Group-based policy optimization methods such as GraphGPO address this by comparing sibling rollouts and using graph representations of trajectories for step-level credit assignment, while critic-free approaches avoid training a separate value network. MCGPO builds on this line of work by constructing matched-context action comparisons from already-collected trajectories.

## [MPMMine Standardizes Benchmarks for Constraint Acquisition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26279) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The MPMMine paper introduces a benchmark suite, dataset, and tooling designed to consistently and reproducibly evaluate constraint acquisition (CA) algorithms for mathematical programming (MP) models. It provides alternative formulations per problem, tens of parameter instantiations per model, thousands of solutions and non-solutions across discrete, continuous, and mixed domains, plus natural-language descriptions, along with the mpmmine-py Python package and an interactive MPMMine Leaderboard. Constraint acquisition research has long been hampered by loosely organized, solver-oriented benchmarks that lack consistent formats and reproducibility details, making fair comparison of CA methods difficult. MPMMine addresses this gap by offering a standardized, extensible, open-format benchmark, which could accelerate algorithmic development and enable more reliable evaluation in the CA and MP communities. MPMMine is guided by design rules covering consistency, standardization, completeness, extensibility, openness, and version control, and it relies on open formats such as MiniZinc, CommonMark, and JSON. The leaderboard runs open-source CA implementations in isolated containers, adapting input/output to their needs to build an extensive, interactive scoreboard.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Constraint Acquisition (CA) refers to algorithms that learn or discover constraints from examples, partial queries, or domain knowledge, often to help build or validate mathematical programming models. Mathematical programming models include linear, integer, nonlinear, and dynamic programming formulations used in optimization. Existing benchmarks were largely designed for comparing solvers rather than CA algorithms, and they often omit details needed for reproducibility, which motivated the creation of MPMMine.

## [Agent Step Value Audits Channel Sensitivity in Black-Box Agent Traces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04419) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.04419v5) defines Agent Step Value (ASV) as a channel-indexed before/after target-margin gain and uses a matched 2x2 design to isolate the state-by-channel interaction in black-box agent traces. Across frozen PubMed question-answering transitions, cross-channel gain signs disagree on 48.3% of comparisons, versus only 0.3% for same-channel retries. The findings show that evaluator-derived step rewards can flip sign depending on which scoring channel is used, meaning process-reward signals reused across evaluation pipelines may be unreliable. This matters for researchers and practitioners building LLM agent benchmarks and reward models, since it suggests step-level rewards need channel-aware auditing before reuse. Transition interactions are nearly balanced in sign (median +0.014; 10% trimmed mean -0.033), but asymmetric negative magnitudes produce a positive direct mean and a negative generated-view mean under the empirical target mixture. A matched 512-token source-contract follow-up shows source-only generation restores the positive mean direction on artifact-bearing retrievals and removes parser-detected support claims from artifact-free before-state views.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Agent evaluation often relies on step rewards derived from an evaluator model to score intermediate actions in a trace. ASV is a replay measurement procedure that takes an observed trace, a fixed candidate set, and an evaluator protocol, then computes how evaluator belief changes at each transition. The paper uses a 2x2 factorial design, a standard experimental structure for estimating main effects and interactions, to separate the effect of agent state from the effect of the scoring channel.

## [Conversational LLM XAI vs Dashboard for UAV Intrusion Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10434) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.10434v3) presents a controlled user study comparing a Large Language Model-powered conversational XAI interface against a traditional visualization dashboard for post-incident auditing of UAV intrusion detection. Results show the conversational interface was perceived as more useful, but it also produced lower appropriate self-reliance, suggesting a risk of over-reliance on AI advice. As LLM-based conversational interfaces become a common way to explain complex security systems, this study highlights a critical trade-off: interfaces that feel more usable may actually weaken operators' tendency to verify evidence when the underlying IDS is wrong. This has direct implications for the design of human-AI collaboration in safety- and security-critical domains such as UAV network defense. The study used a controlled experiment with human participants performing post-incident auditing tasks, comparing the conversational interface and the dashboard on operator understanding, trust, and reliance. The authors suggest the natural-language responses made AI advice easier to accept, reducing verification of underlying evidence, and they conclude by discussing design implications that balance seamless interaction with cognitive forcing functions.

Explainable AI (XAI) aims to make such models transparent, and traditional dashboards are a common visualization approach, but they may struggle to present complex feature relationships clearly. ## [H2Table Models Complex Tables as Hierarchical Hypergraphs for LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01216) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.01216v2) proposes H2Table, a framework that represents complex tables as hierarchical nested hypergraphs rather than linearized sequences. It introduces a tailored hypergraph encoder for message passing between hyperedges (headers) and nodes (cells), plus learnable query vectors that bridge structural embeddings into an LLM, achieving an average 22.88% improvement over state-of-the-art baselines on the HiTab dataset for tables with nesting depth of four. Table reasoning is a persistent weak spot for LLMs because linearization destroys the two-dimensional and hierarchical structure of tables, so a representation that preserves header nesting could substantially improve question answering over real-world spreadsheets and reports. The reported 22.88% gain on deeply nested tables suggests the approach may generalize to other structured-data tasks where higher-order relationships matter. The method relies on a hypergraph encoder that treats headers as hyperedges connecting multiple cells, and on learnable query vectors that act as a lightweight bridge to inject structural embeddings into the LLM rather than modifying the model itself. The abstract is truncated and the evaluation centers on the HiTab dataset, so broader generalization and computational overhead remain to be fully assessed; code is available at https://github.com/lila120/h2table.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models typically process tables by flattening them into text sequences, which loses the two-dimensional layout and the nested header hierarchies common in complex tables. Hypergraph neural networks extend graph learning by letting a single hyperedge connect more than two nodes, making them well suited to modeling higher-order relations such as a header spanning many cells. H2Table combines these ideas, using a hypergraph encoder to capture table structure and learnable query vectors to pass that structure into an LLM.

## [Multi-Harness RL for Coding Agents Shows Modest, Harness-Dependent Gains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04518) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A controlled study (arXiv:2609.04518v2) trains two Qwen3-8B RL variants from the same SFT checkpoint using identical replay data, differing only in whether per-task rewards are normalized within each harness (Within) or across harnesses (Cross). On SWE-bench Verified, multi-harness RL yields estimated mean improvements of only +0.48 (Within) and +0.77 (Cross) percentage points over the SFT baseline across four training harnesses. The results suggest that gains from multi-harness RL are small and do not reliably transfer to unseen execution interfaces, implying that practitioners should not assume RL training on one harness generalizes to another. Since performance differences across harnesses (7.13 percentage points) dwarf training gains, harness and interface design may matter more than the RL algorithm itself. Cross normalization increases the fraction of trajectories with nonzero advantage but provides no consistent advantage over Within on an unseen harness across training seeds and evaluation budgets, and neither rule significantly beats SFT on that unseen harness. After averaging over model variants, the highest and lowest source-harness solve rates differ by 7.13 percentage points, far exceeding the estimated training gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents are language-model-driven systems that solve software issues by generating patches, and they are typically evaluated on SWE-bench Verified, a benchmark of real GitHub issues. An execution harness is the system layer that turns model outputs into concrete actions by orchestrating context, tools, and state, so different harnesses present different interfaces to the same model. Reinforcement learning trains agents on trajectories collected through these harnesses, and reward normalization rescales reward signals (e.g., by subtracting a running mean and dividing by a running standard deviation) to stabilize updates.

## [IBBench-Light Introduces Paired Evaluation Metric PECA for Instruction-Following](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13725) ⭐️ 6.0/10

IBBench-Light introduces paired exact-contract accuracy (PECA), a metric that requires both members of a matched prompt pair to satisfy their output contracts, using twelve semantic bases to yield 144 matched pairs per model across four quantized instruction models (1,152 archived greedy responses). The paper reports that Qwen succeeds on 132 execute and 109 process prompts but only 97 complete pairs, and that in a pinned Phi rerun, changing the end-of-sequence (EOS) set flips exact paired success from 0/144 to 62/144. This work shows that marginal per-prompt accuracy can hide paired failures, meaning standard instruction-following benchmarks may overstate a model's reliability when the same external record must be handled in two different ways. It also demonstrates that generation stopping policy, specifically the EOS configuration, can dramatically change measured results, which matters for anyone comparing or reproducing LLM evaluation numbers. The benchmark audits literal-target exposure and case normalization, and adds 1,722 logged CPU generations to test directive-absent controls, twelve additional semantic bases, within-base wording changes, and generation stopping. A bounded IHEval comparison uses the same SmolLM2 checkpoint and output budget while preserving its published instruction roles and scorer, and the authors stress that task margins and paired counts must be read together with the stopping policy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Instruction-tuned language models are commonly evaluated by asking them to follow prompts and checking whether outputs match expected targets. IBBench-Light focuses on a specific scenario: an external record may be used either as a procedure to apply or as text to read, depending on the user's request, and the benchmark tests both uses against the same record. PECA is a stricter metric than marginal accuracy because it only gives credit when both responses in a pair satisfy their output contracts, and EOS tokens are special tokens that tell a model when to stop generating.

## [Claim-Gated Audit Framework Targets Source Omissions in Generative Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29145) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.29145v2) specifies a claim-gated audit of the query-source-answer tuple for generative search, where an omission is only resolved when relationship evidence, answer adoption, materiality, and disclosure are all observed. A reference checker makes the record contract executable and, on an exhaustive synthetic suite, reproduces all 81 three-state predicate combinations while rejecting 192 deliberately malformed records. Generative search answers can cite a supported passage while still omitting a source relationship that changes how the answer should be interpreted, so this framework offers a formal way to separate citation support from omission resolution. It matters for AI safety and information retrieval because it could give auditors and system builders a versioned, evidence-bound record for flagging unresolved source risk rather than silently treating missing evidence as independence. The specification separates the omission endpoint from citation support and review priority, and binds decisions to versioned evidence spans; incomplete evidence remains unresolved rather than being treated as independence. The authors explicitly caution that the results are finite contract-conformance results, not detector accuracy or evidence of improved user outcomes, and they define independent annotation, held-out evaluation, and paired utility tests still required for semantic validity and deployment benefit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Generative search systems, often built on retrieval-augmented generation, produce natural-language answers with citations to retrieved sources. A recurring risk is that an answer may accurately quote a source passage yet leave out a relationship between sources that would materially change the reader's interpretation. This paper addresses that gap by formalizing an audit over the query-source-answer tuple, using a three-state (true, false, unresolved) predicate logic so that missing evidence is not conflated with a clean result.

A new arXiv paper (2610.06354v2) introduces GraphDecide, a model-independent benchmark that evaluates System One models such as Jev on graph decision tasks across fourteen model-interface configurations. The results show that accurate adjacency recognition does not guarantee broader structural correctness, and that joint graph-text input does not consistently improve prediction. As LLMs are increasingly applied to graph understanding and decision-making, this benchmark provides a standardized way to diagnose whether choice-based System One models actually reason about graph structure or merely recognize local patterns. It matters for researchers and practitioners choosing between native selectors and language-model adapters for graph-related automation. GraphDecide combines structural task profiles, matched graph-text input contrasts, and heuristic-proposal controls, with task contracts, candidate interfaces, and scoring rules that support comparison across native selectors and language-model adapters. Code and aggregate results are available on GitHub at VictorYXL/JevGraphBench.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: System One models are decision models that return typed, calibrated answers instead of free-form text, with Jev being a proprietary example released in limited early access by TypeSafe AI in September 2026. Graph reasoning tasks require models to understand nodes, edges, and structural relationships, which is challenging because graph structure is not naturally expressed in text. GraphDecide fills a gap by benchmarking these choice-based models specifically on graph decision tasks.

## [TypedBench: A New Benchmark for Typed Decision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11392) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces TypedBench, a benchmark built from seven policy-labelled generators and nine evaluation suites to assess calibration, framing sensitivity, and cost in System One decision models that output typed probabilities rather than generated text. It evaluates a hosted model, an open encoder, and open decoders spanning 0.8B–9B parameters on identical items, reporting accuracy as median and range across paraphrases and calibration error relative to a matched perfectly calibrated predictor's finite-sample noise floor. Typed decision models are increasingly used in software that acts on probabilities through thresholds, cost-weighted choices, and escalation rules, so miscalibration or wording sensitivity can trigger unintended actions with no textual rationale for humans to inspect. TypedBench addresses a gap in evaluating non-generative probability-output models, which is relevant for AI safety and reliability in deployment. The hosted model follows the stated policy but is wording-sensitive and systematically underconfident, and under asymmetric costs using its probabilities can be worse than simply taking its top answer; decoders route exactly but are slow as options or questions are added, are least accurate on policy questions, and degrade with more options. The benchmark assesses probability quality through selective prediction, ordinal proper scoring rules, and realised cost under asymmetric cost matrices.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: System One decision models return a typed decision with calibrated confidence instead of generated text, via a non-generative interface that software can act on directly. Calibration refers to how well predicted probabilities match observed outcomes, and selective prediction allows a system to abstain when uncertainty exceeds a threshold. Because these models produce no textual rationale, evaluating their probability quality and robustness to paraphrasing is essential before deploying them in automated decision pipelines.

A new arXiv paper (2505.22184v3) introduces C²TU, a training-free and prompt-free method that uses a Chinese homophone graph and toxic lexicon to detect deliberately disguised toxic words on social media. It offers two filtering variants based on BERT and LLMs, and reportedly outperforms the best competitor by up to 71% in F1 score and 35% in accuracy on two Chinese toxic datasets. Chinese social media platforms face growing challenges from users who evade censorship by replacing toxic words with homophones, and existing detection methods are mostly designed for English. C²TU addresses this gap with a practical, training-free approach that could improve content moderation for Chinese-language platforms without requiring costly model training. The method first uses substring matching against a Chinese homophone graph and toxic lexicon to find candidate toxic words, then filters out non-toxic candidates and corrects cloaks to their toxic counterparts. For the LLM variant, the authors address the auto-regressive limitation in computing word occurrence probability by leveraging the full semantic context of a text sequence.

## [New Measure Theory of Semantic Information Resolves Bar-Hillel-Carnap Paradox](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00525) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper critiques Floridi's theory of strongly semantic information and proposes a measure theory based on the unit circle, analogous to von Neumann's quantum probability, to resolve the Bar-Hillel-Carnap paradox. The approach meets all of Floridi's requirements and finds that contradictory messages are equally informative while contradictions and tautologies have zero informativeness. This work advances the theoretical foundation of semantic information, potentially impacting philosophy of information, logic, and AI by providing a consistent measure that avoids the counterintuitive assignment of maximum informativeness to contradictions. It offers a novel mathematical framework that could inspire further interdisciplinary research. The paper uses the unit circle and von Neumann's quantum probability to construct a measure space for informativeness, ensuring contradictions and tautologies have zero informativeness while contradictory messages are equally informative. An example illustrates the utility of this approach.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: The Bar-Hillel-Carnap paradox arises from the classical theory of semantic information, which inversely relates informativeness to probability, leading to contradictions being maximally informative. Floridi's theory of strongly semantic information (TSSI) attempted to resolve this but failed. This paper builds on those debates, introducing a new mathematical approach inspired by quantum probability.

This paper proposes a preference-conditioned multi-objective reinforcement learning framework that uses Generalized Policy Improvement and Proximal Policy Optimization to learn policies capturing different trade-offs among safety, energy efficiency, and time efficiency for heavy-duty trucks. Evaluated in a scalable highway traffic simulation, it produces smooth, interpretable Pareto fronts and enables transitions between driving behaviors without retraining. Heavy-duty vehicles must balance safety, energy efficiency, and operational costs under demanding commercial requirements, and conventional scalar reward aggregation obscures these trade-offs. The framework explicitly represents three conflicting objectives: safety (collisions and successful completion), energy efficiency (energy cost), and time efficiency (driver cost). A new arXiv preprint (arXiv:2603.02767v5) introduces ITO, a framework for image-text contrastive pretraining that combines multimodal multiple alignment from multi-view image augmentations with a lightweight training-time multimodal fusion module that is discarded at inference. Experiments from millions to billions of image-text pairs show ITO outperforms strong contrastive baselines at moderate scale and yields consistent gains over CLIP under identical data, backbone, and compute at the 100M–1B scale. This work addresses a known limitation of contrastive pretraining—representations organized by modality rather than semantics—while preserving the inference efficiency of standard dual-encoder architectures, making it practically attractive for large-scale multimodal systems. If the gains hold, it could influence how future image-text pretraining pipelines balance training-time regularization against inference cost. The fusion module acts only as a training-time regularizer and is removed at inference, so no extra inference cost is incurred; controlled comparisons indicate the gains are not explained by additional training compute or image augmentation alone. Analysis shows the fusion module's contribution grows with data scale and stabilizes optimization, mitigating late-stage overfitting common in aggressive contrastive learning.

## [IAD-Unify unifies industrial anomaly understanding, segmentation, and generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12440) ⭐️ 6.0/10

IAD-Unify is a unified architecture that links a multimodal language model (Qwen3.5), a dense visual expert (a multi-reference DINOv2 pathway), and a diffusion editor (Stable Diffusion) through task-specific token interfaces. It reports 73.02% MMAD Macro_7, a 57.10% segmentation AP average with one reference, and the lowest Controlled masked DINO distance of 0.3765, alongside a new Anomaly Evidence dataset of 54,501 deduplicated industrial images. Industrial anomaly inspection typically requires separate models for explaining, localizing, and editing defects, so a single shared parameter set that handles all three could simplify deployment and reduce engineering overhead in industrial AI pipelines. The results suggest that understanding, pixel-level segmentation, and controlled generation can be jointly optimized without forcing every task through the same visual bottleneck. The dense anomaly field is compressed from 1,369 cells into 81 structured evidence tokens consumed by Qwen3.5, while 32 task tokens convert them into a semantic residual over the dense mask and a separate 256-query interface resamples Qwen states into Stable Diffusion's cross-attention context. Notably, evidence tokens are excluded from the generation pathway, and training uses staged optimization that initializes dense evidence, aligns it with language, calibrates segmentation, then pretrains and specializes the diffusion editor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Industrial anomaly detection (IAD) is the task of finding and characterizing defects in manufactured products, and it has traditionally been split into separate subproblems such as classification, segmentation, and image generation. DINOv2 is a self-supervised vision transformer that produces universal features for both image-level and pixel-level tasks, while Qwen3.5 is Alibaba's open-source multimodal language model family and Stable Diffusion is a latent diffusion image generator whose cross-attention layers control how text conditions image synthesis. IAD-Unify combines these components so that one model can explain a defect, localize its pixels, and synthesize a controlled edit.

<a id="item-323"></a>
## [EchoDistill boosts noisy-audio robustness in large audio language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23954) ⭐️ 6.0/10

EchoDistill is a noisy-to-clean self-distillation framework that uses clean audio as privileged information during post-training, where a noisy-input student samples candidate responses while a frozen copy of the same backbone processes the corresponding clean audio. Across three LALM backbones and three audio domains at -10dB, it improves average noisy-input accuracy by 1.63 percentage points, raising Qwen2.5-Omni from 59.33% to 62.94% while clean-audio accuracy also rises from 76.56% to 77.56%. Large audio language models remain vulnerable to acoustic noise that obscures task-relevant evidence, so a post-training method that improves robustness without adding inference cost could make speech assistants and audio understanding systems more reliable in real-world noisy environments. Because the gains hold across multiple backbones and domains, the approach may be broadly applicable to existing LALM pipelines. EchoDistill combines masked response-token distillation, task-gated consistency shaping, and teacher-referenced group-relative optimization, and only the student is retained at inference time, introducing no additional inference cost. Replacing matched audio with random, shuffled, or silent inputs reduces accuracy by 3.08-6.42 points, confirming that matched acoustic evidence contributes to predictions, though gains do not reliably extend to non-additive distortions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large Audio Language Models (LALMs) are multimodal systems that integrate audio encoders with language models to jointly process speech, environmental sounds, and music. Self-distillation is a technique in which a model is retrained using its own predictions, often with a frozen or privileged version of itself as a teacher, and group-relative optimization refers to methods like GRPO that use group-relative baselines instead of a value network for policy updates. EchoDistill applies these ideas to noise robustness by treating clean audio as privileged information during post-training.

<a id="item-324"></a>
## [Wall-Clock State Monitors Fail to Detect Moments in Agent Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19386) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A corrected arXiv paper (v2, erratum) retracts a prior 'State Saturation Trap' finding, showing the original result was a pure-accumulator artifact because the affect engine received dt=0 between actions. A pre-registered sweep over 20 trajectories with uniform intervals (dt in 0..600s) reveals that wall-clock-calibrated level triggers have only two regimes: a constant alarm at dt<=1s (20/20, median 18 firings) and silence at dt>=60s, with every critical dt falling in (1,30]s. This work clarifies that runtime monitors for autonomous agents must distinguish sample-time calibration from wall-clock calibration, since agent streams have inter-action times varying by orders of magnitude. It has direct implications for anyone building anomaly detection, drift monitoring, or intervention-timing systems for LLM-based agents. A scriptable gpt-5.4-mini agent loop measures median latency of 1.53s (p90 2.33s), placing real agent cadence inside the trap regime; a minimal wall-clock accumulator over the raw error stream reproduces the same cliff, while a sample-time CUSUM over the identical stream is exactly dt-invariant (20/20). A rising-edge trigger with hysteresis fires only 0-3 times per trajectory in every condition, and transition detection escapes the trap but does not recover human intervention timing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Runtime monitors for autonomous agents often threshold an accumulated internal state, such as a behavioural baseline, drift statistic, or modelled affective state. CUSUM is a classic sequential-analysis change-detection technique that accumulates deviations from a target in sample time, while exponential moving averages (EMAs) and affect models typically use wall-clock half-lives in seconds. On fixed-rate data streams these two calibration styles coincide, but on agent streams where inter-action time varies widely they diverge sharply, producing the behaviour the paper analyses.

## [AD-CERT Combines Adversarial Distillation with IBP for Certified Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31653) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced AD-CERT, a certified training objective that combines adversarial distillation with an Interval Bound Propagation (IBP) upper bound, distilling adversarial information over the logit space from an empirically robust teacher. AD-CERT achieves state-of-the-art certified performance on several robustness benchmarks for ReLU networks, and logit-level distillation improves certified accuracy over robust feature-space distillation by up to 5.40 percentage points. Certified training typically forces a painful trade-off between standard accuracy and verifiable robustness, so a method that improves both simultaneously could make formally verified models more practical for safety-critical deployments. The 5.40 percentage point gain from logit-level distillation also offers a concrete design lesson for future certified training objectives. The method works by interpolating between adversarial training and IBP-based over-approximation, using distilled adversarial logit information as an effective lower bound surrogate for the worst-case loss. Results are demonstrated specifically for ReLU networks, and the code is available at https://github.com/matteo-melis/ad-cert.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Certified training aims to produce neural networks whose predictions can be formally verified against adversarial perturbations, usually by optimizing an upper bound on the worst-case loss over an allowed perturbation set. Interval Bound Propagation (IBP) is a popular but imprecise bounding technique that, curiously, often outperforms more precise bounds in certified training. Adversarial distillation transfers knowledge from a robust teacher model to a student, and this paper repurposes it as a lower-bound surrogate inside a certified training objective.

<a id="item-326"></a>
## [ThinkNet: Validation-Controlled Evolutionary Deep Ensembles for MI-EEG Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33967) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ThinkNet introduces a validation-controlled framework for subject-independent motor-imagery EEG classification that combines train-only normalization, validation-guided evolutionary search, and validation-gated inference, ensuring the held-out test subject never influences preprocessing, model selection, or ensemble policy. On the four-class BCI Competition IV-2a benchmark with nine Leave-One-Subject-Out folds, a validation-selected compact decoder reached 44.35±15.41% accuracy with only 4.9K parameters and 0.99 ms batch-1 Orin CUDA inference, while validation-gated ensembling improved to 43.98±16.25%. The work directly targets a pervasive methodological flaw in EEG-based brain-computer interface research, where test-subject information can leak into normalization, model selection, or ensembling and inflate reported held-out-subject accuracy. By formalizing a validation-controlled protocol and showing that compact models can outperform larger ones under subject shift, it offers a more honest and deployable baseline for assistive and rehabilitative BCI systems that must generalize to new users with limited data and compute. Across a broader search over ten decoder families, compact models (≤25K parameters) achieved higher mean held-out accuracy than mid-size and large alternatives after selected retraining (40.10% vs. 35.09% and 34.78%), and the compact six-family ensemble reached 43.31±15.88%. A non-deployable oracle analysis exposed a 6.1-point family-selection gap and near-zero validation–test correlation, indicating that validation reliability under subject shift remains the key bottleneck rather than architecture choice.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Motor-imagery EEG decoding lets a user control a brain-computer interface by imagining movements, but EEG signals vary greatly across individuals, so decoders must generalize to unseen users. Leave-One-Subject-Out cross-validation is the standard way to estimate this generalization by holding out one subject at a time, yet if that held-out subject's data influences normalization or model selection, the estimate becomes optimistically biased. Deep ensembles combine multiple independently trained neural networks to improve accuracy and robustness, and evolutionary algorithms can search over architectures and inference policies, but both must be constrained to validation data to remain honest.

## [MS-GLA Adds Multi-Scale Temporal Resolution to Gated Linear Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35664) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Multi-Scale Gated Linear Attention (MS-GLA), which distributes attention heads across multiple temporal resolutions so that coarser head groups pool longer token spans for long-range dependencies while finer groups retain local syntactic sensitivity. A learnable, input-dependent fusion layer recombines the head group outputs at each timestep, and the method reports up to 18.9% improvement on recall-intensive tasks and 9.5% lower average perplexity on language modeling benchmarks versus GLA at matched parameter counts. This work targets a core limitation of linear attention models — the fixed-capacity memory matrix that must encode both local and global structure at a single resolution — and shows that multi-resolution decomposition can expand effective memory capacity without increasing per-head state size. If it generalizes, it could make linear-time recurrent models more competitive with softmax Transformers on long-context and recall-heavy workloads. The approach adapts principles from Multi-Scale State-Space Models (MS-SSM) to the gated linear attention setting, and the paper evaluates MS-GLA on language modeling, recall-intensive tasks, and long-context generalization. The headline gains are measured at matched parameter counts, so the improvement comes from architectural decomposition rather than added capacity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Gated Linear Attention (GLA) is a linear-time attention variant that replaces softmax attention with a recurrent memory matrix updated via data-dependent gating, giving it efficient inference and competitive performance against architectures like RetNet and Mamba. Unlike standard softmax attention, which compares every token pair, linear attention compresses history into a fixed-size state, which makes long sequences cheaper but can bottleneck how much local and long-range information a single head can store. Multi-scale attention and multi-scale state-space models address similar issues by processing features at several temporal or spatial scales and fusing them.

## [AgroGround: A 794,850-Example Dataset for Grounded Agricultural Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04425) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced AgroGround, a large-scale dataset and benchmark that automatically converts labels from eight agricultural VQA datasets into region-level annotations for disease lesions and whole objects, producing 794,850 instruction examples. They fine-tuned a shared vision-language model and evaluated it on 1,480 human-verified images, showing that combining recognition and localization instructions raises joint accuracy from 19.2% to 43.3%. Reliable agricultural diagnosis requires both identifying what is present and localizing the evidence, yet most agricultural vision models are evaluated for only one of these tasks. AgroGround provides a benchmark that measures joint correctness of identity and localization, potentially advancing vision-language models for precision agriculture and plant disease diagnosis. Grounding-only fine-tuning reduced recognition accuracy from 51.8% to 29.1%, while adding recognition-and-localization instructions raised it to 72.6%; healthy images provided negative supervision, raising abstention on healthy images to 95.0%. The resulting 2B model exceeded its annotation teacher in grounding F1 on the benchmark and on the external PlantSeg test set, with reinforcement learning improving lesion-level grounding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Visual question answering (VQA) is a task where a model answers natural-language questions about an image, and agricultural VQA datasets carry rich semantic labels but rarely link them to image regions. Grounding refers to the ability of a vision-language model to map textual descriptions to specific visual regions, typically via bounding boxes or segmentation. Adding such region annotations by hand is costly at scale, which motivates automated pipelines like the one used to build AgroGround.

## [LinSlot uses Linear Representation Hypothesis for unsupervised attribute discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10722) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces LinSlot, a probabilistic framework that jointly discovers object and attribute representations from raw images by leveraging the Linear Representation Hypothesis (LRH), which posits that composable concepts are encoded as linearly additive subspaces in slot representations. Unlike prior block-slot attention methods that assume a uniform factorization of objects into attributes, LinSlot connects images, slots (objects), and blocks (attributes) via block attention and optimizes the Evidence Lower Bound (ELBO) of the resulting graphical model, achieving improved DCI scores over state-of-the-art methods. This work advances unsupervised disentangled representation learning by replacing a rigid uniform factorization assumption with a more flexible, theoretically grounded model, potentially improving interpretability and image editing capabilities. It also provides empirical evidence for the Linear Representation Hypothesis in slot space, bridging mechanistic interpretability ideas with object-centric vision models. The architecture incorporates LRH in both object and attribute representation spaces and uses block attention to link attributes to slots, optimizing the ELBO of a graphical model. Experiments on multiple datasets show effective discovery of disentangled representations, empirical support for LRH in slot space, and the ability to perform image editing, with improvements in DCI scores.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Slot Attention is an object-centric learning module that binds visual features into a fixed number of slots representing distinct objects, enabling disentangled scene understanding. Block-slot attention extends this by factorizing each object slot into attribute blocks, but assumes a uniform factorization that may be suboptimal. The Linear Representation Hypothesis (LRH) is the idea that high-level concepts are represented as linear directions or subspaces in neural network representations, a principle from mechanistic interpretability. Disentangled representation learning aims to separate underlying factors of variation into independent latent variables, improving interpretability and controllability.

## [Constitutional Gating and Deterministic Recovery for Multi-Agent LLM Negotiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11542) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.11542) proposes a three-part control stack — a 5-Pillar runtime constitution, a 4-tier swarm architecture, and Cognitive Annealing — to help multi-agent LLM systems negotiate with a stateful adversarial Gatekeeper. In 30 runs using Gemini 2.5 Pro agents against a Claude Haiku 4.5 Gatekeeper, atomic purge plus a canonical strike escaped a compliance deadlock 5/5 times versus 0/5 for LLM-only steering (Fisher p = 0.008). Multi-agent LLM systems waste model calls on polite loops, malformed outputs, and compliance deadlocks, so a deterministic recovery mechanism that adds no extra model cost could make agent-to-agent negotiation more reliable and cheaper in production. The finding that LLM-written strikes failed deterministic pre-flight checks 5/5 times, despite an LLM Monitor approving four, highlights the value of hard gates over purely LLM-based oversight. The constitution and Director made an acceptable framing possible but not reliable (0/5 baseline unlocks versus 1/5 and 2/5 with the constitution), and when the swarm unlocked it did so in one turn with 7-8 calls and about 15k tokens (67-73% below baseline), but cost 17-38% more when it did not. Pre-registered hypotheses on average call and token reduction were not supported, and the Monitor and hard gate did not reduce unlocks but left an audit trail.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems often negotiate with external counterparts that have hidden acceptance conditions, leading to wasted API calls and deadlocks. Constitutional AI refers to giving models an explicit written set of rules to guide behavior, while a schema hard gate validates model outputs against a strict format to prevent malformed responses. Cognitive Annealing is the paper's deterministic recovery routine: it detects deadlock, atomically purges the agent-side context, and sends a canonical recovery message built from the constitution's lexicon at zero model cost.

## [Sera Fuses Degradation Semantics with Temporal Models for Battery Health Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11567) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose Sera, a semantic representation aggregation framework that extracts degradation semantics from battery time series using rule-based knowledge and LLM-based interpretation, then fuses them with temporal model representations via gated aggregation. On a mainstream benchmark, Sera cuts prediction error by up to 37.3% over temporal baselines across multiple prediction horizons. Battery state-of-health forecasting underpins battery management in EVs and energy storage, where nonlinear degradation and cell-to-cell heterogeneity make accurate prediction hard. By explicitly encoding higher-level degradation semantics, Sera improves both accuracy and interpretability, which could support more reliable maintenance and safer battery operation. Sera uses a three-branch architecture that independently learns a temporal representation plus rule-based and LLM-based semantic representations, fused through adaptive gated aggregation. Counterfactual analysis shows prediction responses align with the meanings of key degradation descriptors across tested horizons, though results are limited to a single benchmark and arXiv preprint without peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 12, 04:00

**Background**: Battery state of health (SoH) measures how much capacity and performance a battery retains relative to new, and forecasting its future decline is central to battery management systems. Existing data-driven methods mostly learn from numerical time series with temporal models, leaving higher-level degradation characteristics implicit. Sera draws on battery domain expertise to extract these characteristics as structured semantics, combining hand-crafted rules with large language model interpretation.
